# Epistola 2.0 — progress

What we touched, how each change is covered, and what we noticed but left alone, so that at any
moment we can see where 2.0 stands and check that nothing is uncovered.

The design lives in the contract repo (`epistola-contract`, branch `feature/v2`, ADRs
`docs/adr/0001`–`0005`). The suite plan is `docs/catalog-release-model-v2.md`, still to be rewritten
against those ADRs.

**Coverage legend:** ✅ test exists and was run green · 🟡 test exists, not run since the change ·
❌ no test · ➖ no test needed (docs, deletion, merge).

## Current state (28 October 2026)

- `release/2.0` builds on contract `2.0.0-alpha.3` (published), and CI is green. The version is still
  `1.5.1-SNAPSHOT`; the release process sets 2.0.0.
- In since 16 October:
  - the ADR 0036 fold (one `catalog_versions` table);
  - one install core;
  - the 1.x upgrade as "Published in 1.x" drafts;
  - the 2.0 migration chain written again (#1176);
  - rendering defaults V7 and V8;
  - one kind of release, with provenance (#1192);
  - the collect commit-order fix (#1183, #1198);
  - the architecture review and its three batches of fixes (#1186–#1204).
- Load tests (the external tool `epistola-suite-loadtest`, branch `feat/epistola-2-0`) were run on a
  single node and on three JVMs against one database, with and without a node killed. Nothing was
  lost after the collect fix; on one machine the rate was 7–9k documents a minute.

## Still to do (28 October 2026)

Open work, roughly by size. The "Noticed, not done" notes in the sections below predate this list and
have not all been checked against today's code; item 1 is to do that.

1. **Triage the older "Noticed" notes.** There are about 40 of them in the sections below, most
   written before the fold, the chain rewrite and #1192. Mark each one done, still open (move it
   here), or obsolete.
2. **Catalog → content types, the remaining coupling.** `CatalogBoundaryTest` allows 42 class
   dependencies from `catalog` into templates (13), attributes (7), assets (6), stencils (5),
   themes (5) and fonts (3). They are import, export, relocation and content building calling each
   type's store. Breaking them needs a per-type catalog SPI that each domain implements, the
   pattern `CatalogDraftLifecycle` set. That fits with the per-type apply now in
   `CatalogArchiveResources`.
3. **Tags in the UI and REST for every type.** Generic tags are stored (ADR 0033) but only partly
   surfaced.
4. **"Don't keep every save."** Bound the history a draft keeps instead of one row per save.
5. **#1135, editing by several users at once.** Optimistic locking exists (`revision`); there is no
   presence or merging in the editor.
6. **Channels.** Parked earlier, no design yet.
7. **Leftovers from #97:**
   - undo for column styles;
   - a datatable `verticalAlign` preview in the editor;
   - removing an else branch.
8. **Margin collapse inside keep-together blocks.** V8 collapses block margins like CSS, except
   within keep-together.
9. **REST provenance.** `released_via` and `released_from` show in the UI and MCP only; REST needs a
   contract field (#1192).
10. **The upgrade-pin path in REST.** `releaseCatalogDraft` cannot return the dependencies it pinned
    without a contract field (see the 1.x upgrade section).
11. **The theme and stencil editors on a 409.** They show the "released" message but do not offer the
    next draft (see the freeze-guard section).
12. **Local test tooling:**
    - `:apps:epistola:uiTest` stops at its 5-minute task timeout locally (about 120 tests in); CI
      runs it fully;
    - a fresh worktree needs `pnpm build` before app-level Gradle tasks.
13. **Optional:** run the embedded load test (`docs/loadtesting.md`) through the demo app's UI on 2.0,
    to check the real flow (it is covered by `LoadTestReleaseIT`).

Decided and closed:

- the embedded load-test module stays in the product;
- MCP may deploy and undeploy, but never generate (preview only);
- release channels carry no database check;
- `origin = installed` is gone rather than renamed.

Noticed, not done:

- Create a shared catalog creates the catalog before the move, not in one transaction; a catalog
  created and not moved into stays, empty (recorded in ADR 0042).
- `movedTo` is not in the REST merge preview: that needs a contract field.
- `immutableReferenceCount` is always 0 in 2.0 (the planner reads only drafts); the UI says so
  only when it is not. Counting references held in releases from the index is possible if wanted.

Not generic yet, though no class dependency (ADR 0039, "Where it landed"): SQL naming each type's view
or members in the relocation planner, the upgrade analyser's theme/stencil/font checks, the resource
graph and the catalog list's activity query. Move each to the reference index when next touched, the
planner first. The unified writer (`VersionResources.put`) is not built.

Noticed, not done:

- `ResourceInstallOrderTest` explains the order by stencil renumbering and font variants FK'd to

- `:modules:epistola-core:unitTest` runs out of memory locally in the system-font tests, on
  `release/2.0` as well (checked at `3f7bd21ec`); not caused by the spike.

### The 1.x upgrade cuts no release: "Published in 1.x" drafts (27 October; branch `feat/upgrade-published-draft`)

Both drafts name the highest 1.x release as base (`base_version`), and declare by name, unpinned, every catalog their content uses. ### ADR 0036, step (f): the freeze guard (26 October; branch `feat/versions-f-freeze-guard`)

| What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Where                                                                | Covered                                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A change through the id a draft had before it was released gets `CatalogReleaseIsReleasedException` (409 `catalog-version-released`, "open the next draft") instead of "no such draft": `CatalogReleaseTargeting.resolve(write = true)` for every command                                                                                  | `CatalogReleaseScoped`, `SpringMediator`, `CatalogReleaseExceptions` | ✅ `FrozenVersionWriteTest` (fails before), `CatalogDraftsHandlerHtmxTest` (the UI save: 409 with the type) |
| Every writer of draft content refuses a released version first: `requireWritableVersion` / `Handle.writable` in `DraftResources`, `VariantRows` (insert, update, delete, move), `CodeListStore.recordRefresh`, `CatalogDependencyStore.replace`, the theme and preset settings, `HouseTheme`; ready marks only touch a draft's (`inDraft`) | `CatalogSql` and the writers                                         | ✅ `FrozenVersionWriteTest` (a transaction targeting a released row cannot delete its theme; fails before)  |
| Every SQL write to a version's rows is guarded or on the release path, listed with why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `FrozenVersionWriteGuardTest` (guards), named in the catalog guide   | ✅ fails when a writer's check is removed                                                                   |
| The template editor, on that 409, offers to open the same page in the next draft (the catalog address without its version)                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `editor-boot.js`                                                     | ✅ the server's 409 (handler test); the confirm itself is not browser-tested                                |

Not done: the theme and stencil editors show the 409's message but do not offer the next draft.

### ADR 0036, step (e2): the current release is a view, next is the pointer alone (26 October; branch `feat/versions-e-drop-old`)

| What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Where                                                                                                                                                                           | Covered                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `catalogs.released_version`, `released_fingerprint`, `released_at` dropped; the catalog's current release is `catalog_current_releases` (the highest release cut here), joined into every `Catalog` read (`CATALOG_ROW_COLUMNS`, `CATALOG_RELEASE_JOIN`)                                                    | `V20261026203611__core_current_release_view.sql`, `CatalogSql`, `ListRecentCatalogs`, `ListCatalogsForManagement`, `CatalogUpstreamCheckStore`, `SetCatalogPublicationSettings` | ✅ `DropCatalogReleasesMigrationTest`, `RevisionCollectionTest` (deleting the latest or an older release)                                                 |
| Releasing, deleting a release and restoring a snapshot no longer write a pointer; publishing the current release reads the view                                                                                                                                                                             | `ReleaseCatalogDraft`, `DeleteCatalogRelease`, `RestoreTenantSnapshot`, `PublishCurrentCatalogRelease`                                                                          | ✅ the release, delete, snapshot and publication tests                                                                                                    |
| `catalog_versions.is_next`, its sync trigger, its one-per-catalog index and both checks reading it go; writers set `catalogs.next_version_id` (`setNext`), readers ask `isNext(alias)`; one deferred check across both tables: the next version is a draft of its catalog, an unnamed draft is the next one | the migration, `CatalogReleases` (open, startNext, promote, rename), the deployment, dependency, snapshot and theme readers                                                     | ✅ `DropCatalogReleasesMigrationTest` (both refused), `DraftDeploymentTest` (a hand-over moves the pointer twice in one transaction), `SeveralDraftsTest` |
| The subscribed catalog's installed-version trigger works through the pointer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the migration                                                                                                                                                                   | ✅ the subscribed and install tests                                                                                                                       |

Occurrences (main Kotlin): `is_next` 22 → 3 (a column alias in `CatalogReleases`'s row mapper); `released_version` 6 → 2 (the alias in `CATALOG_ROW_COLUMNS`).

**Not done, a decision for the user:** `catalogs.installed_release_version` (with `installed_fingerprint`, `installed_at`) is not a pointer that can become a view: installing sets it _before_ the release row exists (`CatalogImportRows`, `UpgradeCatalog`), `RecordInstalledRelease` reads it to know which release to record, and its trigger moves a subscribed catalog's next version onto it. Folding it into the rows means reordering the install flow; left as it is.

### ADR 0036, step (e1): catalog_releases is gone (26 October; branch `feat/versions-e-drop-old`)

| What                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Where                                             | Covered                                                                                           |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `catalog_releases`, its mirror trigger and the emptied `catalog_release_dependencies` are dropped; the released rows get the release-order, content-hash and released-at indexes                                                 | `V20261026195907__core_drop_catalog_releases.sql` | ✅ `DropCatalogReleasesMigrationTest` (new)                                                       |
| A release's merge parents name its row (`catalog_release_merges.version_id`, cascade); the publication outbox's FK names the version row by catalog and number (it records what was sent by name), cascade kept                  | the migration, `ReleaseLineage`                   | ✅ `DropCatalogReleasesMigrationTest` (both go with the release), the merge and publication tests |
| Releasing freezes the draft's row with the release record itself (`CatalogDrafts.freeze(FrozenRelease)`), no insert into a second table; merge parents are recorded after the freeze                                             | `CatalogReleases`, `ReleaseCatalogDraft`          | ✅ `OneVersionTableTest`, `SeveralDraftsTest`                                                     |
| Installing inserts the released row directly (origin by `released_by_installation`, as the mirror did); deleting a release deletes its row                                                                                       | `RecordInstalledRelease`, `DeleteCatalogRelease`  | ✅ the install, delete and upgrade tests                                                          |
| Every reader of `catalog_releases` reads the released rows (`state = 'released'`): listings, detail, latest, lineage, identity, same content, deployments, generation, snapshot build and restore, exchange, upgrade, relocation | 40 main files                                     | ✅ the full suite                                                                                 |
| New SQL on `catalog_releases` / `catalog_release_dependencies` is refused                                                                                                                                                        | `DroppedContentTableSqlTest`                      | ✅                                                                                                |

Occurrences (main Kotlin): `catalog_releases` 72 → 0 in SQL (12 left are the MCP tool name `list_catalog_releases`); `catalog_versions` 69 → 128, as every release read now names it; `version_id` 269 → 277. The two release tables' names together: 141 → 128. Deviation, recorded: the publication outbox keeps `catalog_id`/`version`, as the record of what was sent, with its FK to the version row by that natural key rather than a `version_id` column.

### ADR 0036, step (a): one row per version (26 October; branch `docs/adr-0036-one-version-table`)

The `advance` key swap is gone | `CatalogDrafts.freeze`/`startNext`/`retire`, `ReleaseCatalogDraft` | ✅ `OneVersionTableTest` (new; fails before, no `state` column and the draft's row stays a draft), `SeveralDraftsTest` (the next version is a new row now) |

Decided with the ADR: `base_version` and `base_fingerprint` stay beside `base_version_id`, because a release's base may not be installed here. A release's settings are on its row: one cut here keeps its draft's; one recorded from a manifest (migrated, installed) takes the manifest's default theme (resolved through its declarations) and presets (`V20261026170942`, `RecordInstalledRelease`), ✅ `ReleaseSettingsOnRowMigrationTest` (failed before: no settings), `AliasReferenceRoundTripTest` (the installed row's default theme; failed without the fill).

| Deleting a release or a catalog removes rows only; the collector takes what they alone held, past the grace period (`ResourceContentStore.collectUnreferenced`, an unbounded tenant scan in the user's transaction that ignored the grace period, is gone)                     | `DeleteCatalogRelease.kt`, `DeleteCatalog.kt`                                          | ✅ `ResourceContentCollectorTest` (both failed before: the content was gone right after the delete)                                |

**Left for later:** the contract switch is done (`2.0.0-alpha.2`: common tags, the variant description, no `sensitive`); no MigrationLauncher auto-repair is needed:
Flyway 12 records no history row for a non-transactional migration that fails, so the next run of the
migration step runs it again (`FailedMigrationRerunIT`); document retention could join partition maintenance (separate concern, kept as a task of
its own).

### ADR 0033 storage, step 7b: templates stored as JSON (25 October; branch `feat/storage-templates`, on `feat/storage-templates-reads`, not pushed)

| What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Coverage                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| Code migration (frozen, JDBC + JSON): rows to content, own-catalog references relative, data example ids dropped and missing keys derived (`dataExampleKey`, golden vectors), variant descriptions beside the resource, ready marks re-pointed at the variant digest                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `V20261025170700`, `V20261025170800` (views, digests, generation identity, tables dropped)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | ✅ `TemplatesToStorageMigrationTest`, `TemplateStorageConversion` (demo), `DataPreservationMigrationIT`                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Quality findings and load-test runs follow a variant through `release_resources`: they go when no template row lists it any more (the trigger on `template_variants` went with the table)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `V20261025170900` (quality), `V20261025171000` (loadtest)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | ✅ `QualityCascadeIntegrationTest` (new: a variant a release still holds keeps its findings)                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Stencil instances: canonical form (contract ADR 0006) in draft and release; the demo catalog's four templates in another form rewritten (`release.version` 6.4.2)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | demo `templates/*.json`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | ✅ `DemoTemplateReadParityTest` compares without collapsing, draft and release name one content                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| A template names its theme by alias and key (relative for its own catalog); a moved theme rewrites the draft templates that use it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `CatalogResourceRelocation.followTheme`, `TemplateStore.move`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | ✅ `TemplateModelRelocationTest`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| A data example is addressed by its key: the contract save returns the stored examples, the data contract editor takes their ids (`storedIds`), MCP takes a `key` (an `id` that is a valid key too)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `DataContractTabHandler`, `DataContractState`, `template-detail.js`, `DataContractMcpTools`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | ✅ `DataContractState.test.ts`, `DocumentTemplateRoutesTest`, `McpWriteToolsIntegrationTest`                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Contract: `VariantEntry.description`, optional, `NON_EMPTY`, left out of the legacy algorithms                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | contract `feature/common-tags` `7bdba7d` (unsigned, not pushed)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | ✅ contract tests + Kotlin conformance; suite fingerprints unchanged against a local `2.0.0-SNAPSHOT`                                                                                                                                                                                   |

**Measured** (`TemplateReadsPerfTest`, `--rerun`; one tenant, 20 catalogs × 25 templates × 3 variants, each released; median ms, before 7a → after 7a → after 7b): list a catalog's templates 4.31 → 2.82 → 1.36, count 0.94 → 2.06 → 0.54, tenant summaries 10.36 → 11.54 → 7.52, one template 0.83 → 0.97 → 0.96, variants 0.99 → 1.34 → 1.20, a variant's model 0.68 → 1.07 → 1.55, data contract 0.67 → 0.73 → 0.52, editor 1.50 → 1.90 → 2.20, save 2.38 → 2.37 → 4.00.

**Autosave churn:** 20 distinct saves of one variant leave 20 content rows (that variant's content, each save; asserted in `TemplateReadsPerfTest`), down from 40 when the template's content was rewritten with every save. Later: don't keep every save.

**Measured, variant rows** (`TemplateReadsPerfTest`, `--rerun`, same machine and data set; median ms, 7b as committed → variant rows): list a catalog's templates 1.30 → 1.28, count 0.58 → 0.59, tenant summaries 7.46 → 6.59, one template 0.57 → 0.88, variants 0.89 → 1.24, a variant's model 1.49 → 0.79, data contract 0.55 → 0.52, editor 2.44 → 1.51, save 4.26 → 4.62. A first cut took the summaries to 10.99 and the save to 7.26: the unique indexes with `parent_id` appended invited PostgreSQL 18 skip scans, and the store read its variants through the view. The wire and variant rows now have unique indexes of their own (partial on `parent_id`), and the store reads a template's variant rows by its identity.

**Done at the contract switch (`2.0.0-alpha.2`):** the variant description moved from beside its part's resource into `VariantEntry.description` (`V20261026085934`, draft variant rows only; a release that held it beside keeps it so, fingerprinted without it; `current_template_variants` reads either, `V20261026090540`); release settings no longer carry `variantDescriptions`; import takes it from the wire. ✅ `TemplatesToStorageMigrationTest` (fails without the migration), `TemplateWireV8MembersTest` (an imported variant's description round-trips with its fingerprint; fails without the import change). Demo catalog 6.5.0: `simple-letter`'s `nl-nl` variant carries a description.

**Contract `2.0.0-alpha.2`, epistola-contract#97, left for separate work:**

- The contract declares `minHeight` (container), `verticalAlign` (table cells, columns, datatable and its columns) and `columnStyles` (columns); the PDF renderer applies none of them. The editor hides `minHeight` and `verticalAlign` until it does (`style-registry.ts`, `NOT_YET_RENDERED`, ✅ `style-registry.test.ts`); `columnStyles` has no control.
- `cellStyles` is declared, allowing `backgroundColor`, `padding`, `border` and `verticalAlign` per cell; the table inspector's cell controls write `textAlign` as well, so they stay hidden until they match the contract.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- `referenced_paths` was stored per variant and read by nothing; it is not kept (the import test that read it back is gone).
- Optional contract validator warning for a non-canonical stored stencil instance: not built (a received archive's form stays in its release).
- A `release/2.0` development database with releases cut before this step holds bare template contents, which the enveloped reads refuse; no installation has them.

### ADR 0033 storage, step 7a: template reads (25 October; branch `feat/storage-templates-reads`, on `feat/drop-release-hash-backfill`, not pushed)

| What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Where                                                             | Coverage                                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `current_templates`, `current_template_variants`: a draft's rows or a targeted release's content, a variant's model from its lifted content (the default variant's from the template's), a theme resolved through `resolve_catalog_alias` | `V20261025150938__core_template_reads.sql`, `TemplateReads.kt`    | ✅ `TemplateReadsTest`, `DemoTemplateReadParityTest`, `ThemePlanStabilityTest` (template views added) |
| Template queries read a release (`ReleaseReadable`): get, list, count, summaries, variants, variant content, data contract                                                                                                                | `templates/queries/**`, `GetDataContract`                         | ✅ `TemplateReadsTest`                                                                                |
| Every reader of the template tables reads the views (usages, graph, upgrade analyzer, relocation list, quality, load test, getting started, documents); only writes name the tables                                                       | 30 files, `TemplateTableReadsTest` (guard)                        | ✅ core, quality, loadtest suites                                                                     |
| A draft stores a model in its released form (own-catalog references relative, no name qualifiers, written through `TemplateDocument`); every read qualifies it (`TemplateModels`); a move rewrites the models into the destination's form | `TemplateDocumentPreparation`, `CatalogResourceRelocation`        | ✅ `DraftReleaseSameContentTest` (failed before), `TemplateModelRelocationTest`                       |
| `FindFontUsages` finds a template's relative reference to a font of its own catalog (would have regressed: delete was allowed)                                                                                                            | `FindFontUsages`                                                  | ✅ `FontUploadAndDeletionTest` (failed without the fix)                                               |
| REST template reads go through the queries with `readRef` (paged in SQL); release mappers and `pageOfRelease` removed. MCP `list_templates`, `get_template`, `list_variants`, `get_variant`, `get_data_contract` take a release           | `EpistolaTemplateApi`, `TemplateMcpTools`, `DataContractMcpTools` | 🟡 full suite                                                                                         |

**Measured** (`TemplateReadsPerfTest`, `--rerun`; one tenant, 20 catalogs of 25 templates of 3 variants, each released; median ms before → after): list a catalog's templates 4.31 → 2.82, count 0.94 → 2.06, tenant summaries 10.36 → 11.54, one template 0.83 → 0.97, variants 0.99 → 1.34, a variant's model 0.68 → 1.07, data contract 0.67 → 0.73, editor 1.50 → 1.90, save 2.38 → 2.37. A first cut took the summaries to 137 ms: joining `catalogs` to the view on tables without statistics made the planner rescan the view per catalog. Catalog keys are now read row by row, as for the other types; a fenced (`OFFSET 0`) draft branch was tried and made the variant reads 10 ms (the fence keeps the template filter out), so the draft branches join plainly.

**For step 7b (decide there):**

- A draft keeps what the wire form does not: a variant's `description`, a data example's `id` (a release reads its key instead), per-variant timestamps and revisions. A draft's data examples may also have no `key` until they are rewritten (a release derives one). Keeping these beside `resource` in the envelope is the obvious place.
- An installed release keeps the archive's form of a stencil instance (`parameterSchemaSnapshot`, no fill slots), while its draft stores it collapsed (`StencilInstances.collapse`), so for the demo catalog draft and release models differ though they mean the same; the parity test collapses before comparing. 7b has to pick one stored form for both (collapse on receive, or keep what arrives).
- `CatalogContentBuilder`, `ImportTemplates`, the move planner, `CatalogDraftCommands` and `ReleaseTargetResolver` still read the tables (listed in `TemplateTableReadsTest`); they change with the writes.
- Existing draft models stay qualified until 7b's conversion stores them relative; reads qualify both, so nothing reads differently meanwhile.

### Drop the release content-hash backfill (25 October; branch `feat/drop-release-hash-backfill`, on `feat/storage-images`, not pushed)

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                                                                                                             | Coverage                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| `ReleaseContentHashBackfill` and its task removed; `ReleaseIdentity` reads the recorded hash only (no on-the-spot computation, no `record`)                                                                                                                                          | `ReleaseIdentity`                                                                                                 | ✅ `ReleaseIdentityTest`                            |
| Every writer records the hash: cutting (`ReleaseCatalogDraft`, which the upgrade runner's first release uses) and receiving or installing (`RecordInstalledRelease`, which the subscribed-catalog record and a snapshot restore use); so `catalog_releases.content_hash` is NOT NULL | `V20261025143621`                                                                                                 | ✅ full suite, `OneXUpgradeReleasesIT`              |
| Callers simplified: a dependency pin, a merge parent's lineage, `releaseNamed`, `travelling`, `ListReleasesWithSameContent` (no `pending`), the release page (no "being computed")                                                                                                   | `CatalogDependencyStore`, `ReleaseCatalogDraft`, `ReleaseIdentity`, `ListReleasesWithSameContent`, `release.html` | ✅ release suites, `CatalogReleaseIdentityHtmxTest` |
| 1.x upgrade: a 1.x release is gone, each catalog has its first 2.0 release (the patch after its 1.x one) with a hash, no backfill task is scheduled                                                                                                                                  | `OneXUpgradeReleasesIT`                                                                                           | ✅                                                  |

A stale scheduled-task row of the removed task (`core.release-content-hash`) on an upgraded
development database is deleted by `ClusterScheduledTaskReconciler` once no node carries it.
