| Upload, import (put), delete, move through the store; image reads release-readable; REST list and download read a release the same way, paged in SQL; REST upload has no `sensitive` (contract `2.0.0-alpha.2`)                               | asset commands and queries, `EpistolaImagesApi`      | ✅ asset, REST suites                                            |
| Reaper: only `resource_binaries` holds a blob                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `ContentReaper`                                      | ✅ `AssetContentStoreIT`, `ContentReaperMaintainerIT`            |
| Leftovers: `font-face-asset` edge kind and its test, the released-resource REST mappers for stencils, fonts and images, `GetReleasedBinary` removed                                                                                           | `CatalogDependencyCycles`, `ReleasedResourceMappers` | ✅                                                               |
| Conversion parity: system images and uploaded ones                                                                                                                                                                                            | `ImageStorageConversion`                             | ✅ `ImageStorageTest`                                            |

**Measured** (`ImageStoragePerfTest`, `--rerun`; one tenant, 20 catalogs of 25 SVGs, each released;
median / p95 of 200 runs, upload 100; ms):

| Operation                            | before       | after        |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------ | ------------ |
| list a catalog's images (page of 50) | 0.85 / 1.61  | 1.03 / 2.33  |
| list a tenant's images               | 8.95 / 10.55 | 8.25 / 10.39 |
| read one image                       | 0.39 / 0.46  | 0.51 / 0.67  |
| read one image's bytes               | 0.65 / 0.77  | 0.98 / 1.43  |
| upload an image                      | 3.45 / 4.08  | 5.67 / 7.69  |

An upload stores content, its link and the row besides the bytes (+2 ms); a read looks the bytes up by
hash after the row (+0.3 ms).

**#751 close-out:** the `sensitive` scope is gone rather than surfaced. It was never on the UI, REST
or catalog formats; the suite stores every image's bytes once per installation, and the contract
drops the REST field on `feature/drop-image-sensitive`.

**Noticed, left alone:** a deleted image's bytes now wait for the content collector (a day's grace)
to take its content before the reaper can take them. Done in step 8: the collector takes both.

### ADR 0033 storage, step 6a: the 1.x content store (25 October; branch `feat/storage-images`, on `feat/storage-fonts`, not pushed)

The user's decision: `content_store` was always database-only, so instead of a preflight stop the
1.x content backfill (#738) becomes a code migration and the runtime job goes.

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                                                          | Coverage                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Remaining 1.x blobs move in the migration step, a batch per transaction, idempotent: documents per month into `document_content`; assets without a hash into `asset_content` (shared scope, tenant's if sensitive), hash stamped; missing bytes logged and left                      | `V20261025110000`                                              | ✅ `LegacyContentMigrationTest` (SHA-256 golden vector; sensitive, missing, orphan cases)    |
| A face with no hash of its own whose bytes are still in `content_store` is hashed by the fonts conversion (`V20261025105724`) itself, so it joins its font; the content-store migration runs after fonts (`V20261025110000`), because a migration must be newer than the merged ones | `V20261025110000`                                              | ✅ `LegacyContentMigrationTest` (a 1.x face and asset without hash, failed on the old order) |
| `content_store` dropped, orphan rows counted and logged first                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `V20261025133400`                                              | ✅ `LegacyContentMigrationTest`, `DataPreservationMigrationIT`                               |
| `ContentBackfillRunner`, `LegacyBlobFallback`, the legacy `ContentStore` and its five backends, their bean, settings and tests removed; an asset without a hash reads as having no content                                                                                           | `storage/`, `GetAssetContent`, `EpistolaDocumentGenerationApi` | ✅ storage and asset suites                                                                  |

`ReleaseContentHashBackfill` still runs after startup; recorded in ADR 0035, and queued to go in its own
step (`feat/drop-release-hash-backfill`).

### ADR 0033 storage, step 5: fonts (25 October; branch `feat/storage-fonts`, on `feat/storage-stencils`, not pushed)

Fonts before images, as decided: a face's file becomes the font's own binary, so `assets` holds
images only once this step is in. The bundled system faces go into `asset_content` once per
installation (decision (i)); the classpath fallback (`bundledFace`, `classpath_location`,
`withBundledFaces`) is gone.

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Where                                                   | Coverage                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Content `{ resource: <FontResource> }` (faces by italic, then weight, each with mediaType and contentHash) named by a draft row; read through `current_fonts`                                                                                                  | `FontStore`, `V20261025105724`, `V20261025105800`       | ✅ `FontStorageTest`, `DraftReleaseSameContentTest`             |
| A face's file is a binary of the font: `ResourceContentStore` links every stored image or font content to its bytes (`resource_binaries`, tenant scope first, else shared), drafts as releases; no asset row for a face                                        | `ResourceContentStore.linkBinaries`, `FontStore`        | ✅ `FontStorageTest`, `SystemFontsIntegrationTest`              |
| `ImportFont` takes each face's bytes (`ImportFontVariant(weight, italic, mediaType, content)`): upload (UI, MCP), catalog install, system seeding; checks weight, media type, 5 MB, one face per weight/italic                                                 | `ImportFont`, `FontHandler`, `FontMcpTools`, installers | ✅ font suites, `FontUploadHandlerTest`, MCP suite              |
| System fonts: files read and hashed once per JVM, bytes written once per installation (shared scope), a family whose content is current writes nothing; contents stored before their files (the conversion) are linked on seeding                              | `EnsureSystemFonts`, `FontStore.linkStoredFaces`        | ✅ `SystemFontsIntegrationTest`, `SystemFontBootPerfTest`       |
| The boot pass validates the system catalog only when it or its fonts changed (validating 14 MB of faces every boot cost ~100 ms a tenant)                                                                                                                      | `InstallSystemCatalog`                                  | ✅ full suite                                                   |
| Reads are release-readable: `ListFonts`, `GetFontVariants`, `ResolveFontFace`; REST drops its release branch and the bundled-face patch-up                                                                                                                     | font queries, `EpistolaFontApi`                         | ✅ REST suite                                                   |
| Export from the content (bundled faces included, as a release keeps them); a face's bytes are read when an archive asks for them, not on every build                                                                                                           | `ExportFonts`, `CatalogContentBuilder`                  | ✅ export suites                                                |
| Migration never refuses: face-only asset rows (not an image, named by nothing but a face, bytes in `asset_content`) go; an image a face used, a font file an image node names, and a file whose bytes the 1.x content backfill has not moved yet stay (logged) | `V20261025105800`                                       | ✅ `FontsToStorageMigrationTest`, `DataPreservationMigrationIT` |
| Conversion parity: system fonts and an uploaded one                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `FontStorageConversion`                                 | ✅ `FontStorageTest`                                            |

**Measured** (`FontStoragePerfTest`, `--rerun`; one tenant, 10 catalogs of 10 families of two
uploaded faces, each released, beside the system fonts; median / p95 of 200 runs, save 50; ms):

| Operation               | before         | after         |
| ----------------------- | -------------- | ------------- |
| list a catalog's fonts  | 0.88 / 1.10    | 0.95 / 1.16   |
| list a tenant's fonts   | 1.81 / 2.11    | 1.79 / 2.08   |
| read a font's faces     | 27.08 / 30.67  | 0.69 / 1.01   |
| resolve a face's bytes  | 26.60 / 29.88  | 0.61 / 0.80   |
| save a font (two faces) | 98.12 / 106.39 | 62.52 / 66.75 |

Reading faces was slow because each face found its file through the `assets` join over every
catalog's current version; it is now one row's JSON.

**Boot cost** (`SystemFontBootPerfTest`, its own fresh database, `--rerun`; ms):

| Operation                                                                       | before | after |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ----- |
| create the first tenant (fresh installation: files read, hashed and written)    | 1082   | 1848  |
| create a second tenant                                                          | 213    | 477   |
| re-seed the system fonts of an existing tenant (median of 20)                   | —      | 16.9  |
| boot pass of an existing tenant's system catalog, fonts included (median of 20) | 34.6   | 29.7  |

A tenant's first install now validates and records a system catalog whose fonts carry their 36
faces (14 MB): +260 ms a tenant, +770 ms for the first, which also writes the files. Every later
boot is cheaper than before.

**Stop needed, not built: the 1.x content backfill.** An installation that upgrades from a 1.x
version before the content backfill finished (`ContentBackfillRunner`, #738) has assets whose bytes
are still in the legacy `content_store`, possibly on S3 or a filesystem, which Flyway cannot reach.
This step keeps such a face's asset row (it is the only way to its bytes) and its face names the hash
1.x recorded, but nothing links the font to the bytes once the backfill moves them; the images step
cannot convert such an image at all (an image's content names its hash). Recommendation: an upgrade
preflight stop (ADR 0035): refuse 1.x → 2.0 while any asset has no `content_hash`, telling the
operator to let 1.x finish its content backfill first.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- `CatalogDependencyCycles.NOT_A_CATALOG_DEPENDENCY` still names `font-face-asset`, an edge kind the
  graph no longer reports. Done in step 6.
- `FontResource.toDto(at)` (`ReleasedResourceMappers`) is unused now that REST reads releases
  through the queries. Done in step 6.

### ADR 0033 storage, step 4: stencils (25 October; branch `feat/storage-stencils`, on `feat/storage-attributes`, not pushed)

Built against contract `2.0.0-alpha.1`: stencil tags stay `StencilResource.tags`. The move to a
common `tags` on every resource is its own commit, after the next alpha.

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Where                                                        | Coverage                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Content `{ resource: <StencilResource> }` named by a draft row; what its document uses (stencils, images, fonts, a theme) is the wire form's alias and key, indexed in `resource_references` when stored                  | `StencilStore`, `V20261025083103`, `V20261025083200`         | ✅ `StencilStorageTest`                                              |
| Reads through `current_stencils` (`catalog_version_resources('stencil')`, fenced lateral payload); list, get and count are `ReleaseReadable`; render reads (`StencilResolvers`) read another draft through `VersionReads` | `Stencil.kt`, `StencilResolvers`, queries                    | ✅ `StencilStorageTest`, `DemoStencilReadParityTest`, stencil suites |
| Tags filtered in SQL over the JSON (`tags @> jsonb_build_array(:tag)`), no GIN index; a tag is bound as a value, so a quote no longer breaks the query                                                                    | `ListStencils`, `CountStencils`                              | ✅ `StencilStorageTest` (quote case was a SQL error before)          |
| A release's stencil description is the content's, not the row's                                                                                                                                                           | `current_stencils`                                           | ✅ `DemoStencilReadParityTest` (failed before: see noticed)          |
| Writes: create, update (locked, merged onto what is there), delete, import (put), ready mark (content hash)                                                                                                               | commands, `ImportStencil`, `MarkStencilReady`                | ✅ stencil suites, `DataPreservationMigrationIT`                     |
| Relocation: a stencil rewrite and move go through the store; a rewrite keeps `updated_by`                                                                                                                                 | `CatalogResourceRelocation`, `MovableResource`               | ✅ `CatalogResourceRelocationIntegrationTest`                        |
| "Used by" (dangling instance check), discard holders, upgrade analyzer, graph, export, browse, management, relocatable, purge on the view or the rows                                                                     | `StencilReferences`, `CatalogDraftCommands`, catalog queries | ✅ catalog, graph and relocation suites                              |
| REST: the release branch of list/get/content is gone, a release reads and pages in SQL; MCP `list_stencils`/`get_stencil` take a release number                                                                           | `EpistolaStencilApi`, `StencilMcpTools`                      | ✅ REST/MCP suites                                                   |
| Conversion parity: the frozen migration converts each stencil to the hash and references the suite stores                                                                                                                 | `StencilStorageConversion`                                   | ✅ `StencilStorageTest`, `DemoStencilReadParityTest`                 |
| Plan stability with stencils accumulating                                                                                                                                                                                 | `ThemePlanStabilityTest`                                     | ✅                                                                   |

**Measured** (`StencilStoragePerfTest`, `--rerun`, M1 Pro, Postgres 17; one tenant, 20 catalogs of
25 stencils (30 text nodes, two of five tags each), each released; median / p95 of 200 runs; ms):

| Operation                              | before       | after         |
| -------------------------------------- | ------------ | ------------- |
| list a catalog's stencils (page of 50) | 7.56 / 8.48  | 4.71 / 5.76   |
| list a tenant's stencils (page of 50)  | 9.34 / 16.15 | 12.87 / 15.28 |
| search a tenant's stencils by name     | 4.39 / 10.86 | 4.30 / 4.79   |
| filter a tenant's stencils by tag      | 8.12 / 12.51 | 11.52 / 12.36 |
| read one stencil                       | 1.04 / 1.56  | 0.84 / 1.05   |
| save a stencil                         | 3.71 / 4.56  | 6.51 / 11.17  |

The tenant-wide list and the tag filter read every current stencil's payload before sorting (the
fenced lateral cannot be deferred past the `ORDER BY`): +3.5 ms at 500 stencils. A tag filter at 12 ms
does not yet justify a GIN index. A save stores content, references and the row: +2.8 ms.

**Follow-up 1 (25 October): a draft stores what its release stores.** A stencil referring to its
own catalog's resources was stored qualified in the draft and relative in the release, so the two
named different content. The store now writes the released form (`CatalogContentBuilder.Outbound`:
own-catalog references relative, no name qualifiers), and the conversion does the same; the stencil
reads (`Stencil.asRead`) and the draft render read (`StencilResolvers`) qualify them against the
stencil's catalog, so the editor, rendering, relocation and imports see what they always saw, and no
editor or rendering code changed. A relocation rewrite compares the stored document; a move reads
qualified and stores in the target's form. Checked the other JSON types: themes, code lists and
attributes already named one content (`DraftReleaseSameContentTest`, which failed for the stencil
only before). The demo parity test now also compares the content hashes of draft and release.

**Follow-up 4 (25 October): `ThemePlanStabilityTest` holds a plan shape, not a time.** It failed
once under full-suite load (448 to 968 ms). It now prepares each read of the four JSON types (a
catalog's, and one by key) and takes its generic plan (`plan_cache_mode = force_generic_plan`) on a
never-analyzed database, once while only the system catalog is stored and once after 5 catalogs have
grown, and requires no nested loop with `resource_contents` outside and `release_resources` inside
(the plan that went wrong: the tenant's rows rescanned per stored content). A copy of the theme read
before the fix (the two-union view joined to content and catalog) shows that plan, so the check
fails on the old shape. Timing stays in the perf tests.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- The demo catalog's index (`catalog.json`) describes `company-header` differently from the
  stencil's own file ("with logo placeholder and company name" against "with company name and
  separator"). A release row carried the index's wording; reads now take the content's. Fixing the
  catalog means a `release.version` bump and fingerprint, outside this step.
- `StencilResource.tags` is serialized when empty (`[]`), unlike `readsTemplateData`. The common
  `tags` on the contract branch (`feature/common-tags`, `75aba71`, not pushed) keeps that for
  stencils and leaves empty tags out only on the types that had none, so no stored content hash
  and no fingerprint moves and the switch commit needs no code migration.
- Follow-up: tags for the other types in UI, REST and MCP.
- Templates have the same draft/release divergence as stencils had (`TemplateDocumentPreparation`
  qualifies, the release relativizes): step 7.

**Follow-up 3 (25 October): a stencil of another catalog, rendered from a release.** Proven wrong:
`CrossCatalogStencilFontRenderTest` (a template of A inlines a stencil of B whose text uses B's own
font, relatively) drew the text in Liberation Sans from A's release, Inter from the draft. Release
content is relative, and the release render (`ReleaseRenderSource.expandStencils`) and the
dependency-release path of `StencilResolvers` inlined it unqualified, so B's font was looked up in A.
Both now qualify the stencil's document against its catalog (`qualifiedIn`), as the draft path does;
the test passes for the release and the draft. A bug since releases relativized, not from step 4.

- `ContentReferences.of` reads an attribute's `codeListBinding` at the payload's top level, which an
  enveloped attribute does not have; the `CODE_LIST_BINDING` site finds it instead, so the branch is
  dead code.

### ADR 0033 storage, step 3: variant attributes (25 October; branch `feat/storage-attributes`, on `feat/storage-code-lists`, not pushed)

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                 | Where                                                                                                 | Coverage                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Content `{ resource: <AttributeResource> }`; the binding is the wire form's alias and key, indexed in `resource_references`, no foreign key (option A); a write refuses a missing list | `AttributeStore`, `V20261025072712`, `V20261025072800`                                                | ✅ `AttributeStorageTest`                                                             |
| One alias resolver `resolve_catalog_alias` and `catalog_version_references(type)`; in use (`DeleteCodeList`), discard holders, upgrade analyzer and graph read through it              | `V20261025072800`, `CatalogDraftCommands.holders`, `CatalogUpgradeAnalyzer`, `GetTenantResourceGraph` | ✅ `AttributeStorageTest`, `SeveralDraftsTest`, relocation and graph suites           |
| A binding to another catalog reads it at the declared version (a pinned release), for validation and pickers                                                                           | `BoundCodeListVersion`, `CodeListEntryExists`, `ListCodeListEntries`, `AttributeOption`               | ✅ `AttributeStorageTest` (pinned case; fails before: the draft's entry was accepted) |
| Relocation: `CODE_LIST_BINDING` reference site; a moved or renamed code list rewrites draft attributes; a moving attribute pins its own-catalog binding; attributes move as draft rows | `ResourceReferenceSites`, `CatalogResourceMovePlanner`, `CatalogResourceRelocation`                   | ✅ `CatalogResourceRelocationIntegrationTest` (now one rewrite)                       |
| Golden vectors for the reference walker; the theme conversion's frozen walker agrees                                                                                                   | `fixtures/references/v1.json`                                                                         | ✅ `ReferenceWalkerGoldenVectorsTest`                                                 |
| Reads are release-readable: REST drops its release branch, MCP takes a release number                                                                                                  | `EpistolaTenantApi`, `AttributeMcpTools`                                                              | ✅ REST/MCP suites                                                                    |

**Measured** (`AttributeStoragePerfTest`, `--rerun`, M1 Pro, Postgres 17 on tmpfs; one tenant, 20
catalogs of 5 code lists (50 entries) and 15 attributes (a third bound in-catalog, a third bound to
the first catalog's lists, a third inline), each released; median / p95 of 200 runs; ms):

| Operation                          | before        | after         |
| ---------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------- |
| list a tenant's attributes         | 53.55 / 58.63 | 9.30 / 11.67  |
| list a catalog's attributes        | 3.36 / 4.00   | 1.58 / 2.23   |
| read one attribute                 | 0.83 / 1.03   | 1.02 / 1.19   |
| save an attribute                  | 9.27 / 12.64  | 5.65 / 8.37   |
| validate variant attributes (3)    | 54.72 / 59.24 | 13.19 / 17.93 |
| delete a bound code list (refused) | 1.64 / 2.36   | 13.33 / 14.68 |

The tenant-wide list (and every variant validation, which reads it) got faster because catalog names
come from one read per statement instead of joins. The in-use check on deleting a code list costs
13 ms (was 1.6): it walks the current attributes' references and resolves aliases, where a foreign key
answered by identity; it runs only on delete.

**Follow-up (25 October):** the resolver's third fallback, "the tenant's one catalog of that key",
is gone: an alias nothing declares resolves to nothing. The only case that needed it was 1.x data
between the migration and the 2.0 upgrade, which declares a 1.x catalog's dependencies when it runs
(`DataPreservationMigrationIT` failed without it). The attribute conversion now declares the catalog
of a list an attribute of another catalog binds, unpinned and under its key as the upgrade would, so
the alias means what the identity meant from the first moment. A relocation rewrite of an attribute
keeps its previous `updated_by` (`CatalogResourceRelocationIntegrationTest`).

**Noticed, left alone:**

- Renaming a dependency's alias is not possible today: every dependency is declared under its key
  (`UpdateCatalogDependencies` takes no alias). When it comes, it goes through the relocation
  rewrite, not a mechanism of its own (ADR 0033).
- Concurrency of the in-use check: a code list deleted while an attribute binding it is saved in
  another transaction is not prevented (no lock); a general approach to several people working on
  one catalog is a later decision.

### ADR 0033: one content shape for every type (24 October; branch `feat/storage-code-lists`, on #1134, not pushed)

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                                                                                                  | Coverage                                                                       |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Themes are stored as `{ "resource": <ThemeResource> }` too (save, draft start, release cut and received); `current_themes` reads through `resource`; the theme code migration (unreleased) edited in place to write the envelope, checksum re-pinned | `ThemeStore`, `StoredContent.ENVELOPED_TYPES`, `V20261024110723`, `V20261024114440`, `V20261024114500` | ✅ `ThemeStorageTest`, `ThemeStorageConversion`, `DataPreservationMigrationIT` |
| `StoredContent.resourceOf` refuses a content without a `resource` object; the release assembler unwraps by the row's type; `current_code_lists` no longer falls back to a bare content                                                               | `StoredContent`, `ReleaseContentAssembler`, `V20261024174200`                                          | ✅ `StoredContentShapeTest` (2)                                                |

**Noticed, left alone:** a `release/2.0` development database with releases cut before these commits
holds bare theme and code list contents, which `resourceOf` now refuses; 2.0 is unreleased, so no
installation has them, and no migration rewraps them.

### ADR 0033 storage, step 2: code lists (24 October; branch `feat/storage-code-lists`, stacked on #1133, not pushed)

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Where                                                                                                                               | Coverage                                                                                                                 |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Content `{ resource, source }` per version, draft and release alike; export and the fingerprint use `resource`; every reader takes the released form through `StoredContent.resourceOf` (themes too, so moving them to the envelope changes their writer only)                                                                                           | `CodeListStore`, `StoredContent`, `ResourceContentStore.retain` (settings beside), `ReleaseCatalogDraft`, `ReleaseContentAssembler` | ✅ `CodeListStorageTest`, `CodeListReadParityTest`                                                                       |
| Reads through `catalog_version_resources('codeList')` and `current_code_lists`; `GetCodeList`/`ListCodeLists`/`ListCodeListEntries` read a release; REST loses its release branch, MCP takes a release number, `…@1.2.0/code-lists/{key}` opens read-only; every other reader (attributes, export, graph, browse, relocation, upgrade analyzer) switched | 20 readers                                                                                                                          | ✅ `ReleasedCodeListPageHtmxTest`, core/app/MCP/REST suites                                                              |
| Entries only in the content: list, exists and hide in SQL over the jsonb (hide rewrites the content)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `ListCodeListEntries`, `CodeListEntryExists`, `UpdateCodeListEntryHidden`                                                           | ✅ `CodeListStorageTest`                                                                                                 |
| `resource_credentials` (named by id, encrypted, rotation in place, collector removes unnamed ones); `code_list_refreshes` (per draft, copied with the draft)                                                                                                                                                                                             | `V20261024174100`, `CoreEncryptedCredentialColumns`, `ResourceContentCollector`                                                     | ✅ `CodeListCredentialRotationTest`, `CodeListStorageTest`                                                               |
| Refresh writes only the refresh when the entries hash the same                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `RefreshCodeList`                                                                                                                   | ✅ `RefreshCodeListTest` (+1)                                                                                            |
| Code migration `V20261024174143` (pinned checksum); ready marks to content hash; tables dropped                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `db/migration/core`, `V20261024174200`                                                                                              | ✅ `CodeListStorageConversion` both ways (system lists, a sourced list with a credential), `DataPreservationMigrationIT` |
| Plan stability covers code lists; guards refuse SQL on the old tables                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `ThemePlanStabilityTest`, `DroppedContentTableSqlTest`                                                                              | ✅                                                                                                                       |

**Measured** (`CodeListStoragePerfTest`, `--rerun`, M1 Pro, Postgres 17 on tmpfs; one tenant with 20
catalogs of 10 lists of 20 entries, released, plus a list of 1,000 and one of 10,000 entries; median /
p95 of 200 runs, hide 100; ms; before = the tables, after = JSON):

| Operation                    | before        | after         |
| ---------------------------- | ------------- | ------------- |
| list a tenant's code lists   | 5.75 / 6.32   | 8.30 / 11.02  |
| 1000: read the code list     | 0.77 / 0.94   | 0.90 / 1.08   |
| 1000: list visible entries   | 2.42 / 3.09   | 2.55 / 2.78   |
| 1000: list all entries       | 2.32 / 2.73   | 2.43 / 2.77   |
| 1000: entry exists           | 0.86 / 1.14   | 0.75 / 0.84   |
| 1000: entry does not exist   | 0.76 / 0.96   | 0.74 / 0.84   |
| 1000: hide or show an entry  | 4.29 / 5.77   | 8.81 / 10.26  |
| 10000: read the code list    | 0.82 / 1.26   | 1.71 / 1.92   |
| 10000: list visible entries  | 12.95 / 15.98 | 17.74 / 19.30 |
| 10000: list all entries      | 13.94 / 14.85 | 16.30 / 17.30 |
| 10000: entry exists          | 0.79 / 0.98   | 1.57 / 1.73   |
| 10000: entry does not exist  | 0.83 / 1.12   | 1.84 / 2.26   |
| 10000: hide or show an entry | 4.05 / 4.67   | 44.90 / 49.41 |

There is no search or paging API for entries; the list reads every entry, as before. Reading one list
reads its whole content (its source is in it), so it grows with the entries (1.7 ms at 10,000). **Hiding
one entry of a 10,000-entry list takes 45 ms (was 4 ms)**: it rewrites and hashes the whole content
(about 500 KB); at 1,000 entries 8.8 ms (was 4.3). Not indexed, as decided; lists are typically
hundreds of entries.

**Noticed, left alone:** the REST read of a released code list now reports its real source type
(it said INLINE for every release). `UpdateCodeList` with no credential clears it, as the column did.

### ADR 0033 storage, step 1: themes (24 October; branch `feat/storage-themes`, not pushed)

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Where                                                                | Coverage                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One read place: SQL function `catalog_version_resources(type)` (the targeted draft's rows, or the targeted release's: settings `epistola.release_catalog`/`release_version`) and per type `current_themes`; one mapper `ThemeContent`; `VersionReads.at` for reading another version than the message's | `V20261024110723`, `VersionReads.kt`, `ThemeRows.kt`                 | ✅ `ThemeReadParityTest` (fails before: release not found), `DemoThemeReadParityTest`                                                                         |
| `GetTheme`/`ListThemes` read a release (`ReleaseReadable`, `DraftRef.Released`); every theme reader switched (usages, graph, editor context, templates, export, upgrade analyzer, relocation, purge, stats)                                                                                             | theme queries, `SpringMediator`, 20 readers                          | ✅ core, app, MCP, REST suites                                                                                                                                |
| REST theme reads lose the release branch; MCP `get_theme`/`list_themes` take a release number; `…@1.2.0/themes/{theme}` opens read-only                                                                                                                                                                 | `EpistolaThemeApi`, `ThemeMcpTools`, `CatalogDraftBar`               | ✅ `ReleasedThemePageHtmxTest`, `CatalogReleaseMcpToolsIntegrationTest`                                                                                       |
| Draft rows: `release_resources.version_id`, identity/revision/updated_at/touch triggers on draft rows, `copy_catalog_version_content` copies them, digests from `content_hash`, index `(tenant, catalog, type)` for drafts                                                                              | `V20261024110723`, `V20261024114500`, `DraftResources`, `ThemeStore` | ✅ `ThemeStorageTest` (5), `DeletionDriftTest`, `SeveralDraftsTest`                                                                                           |
| Code migration `V20261024114440` converts every theme row (JDBC + JSON tree, pinned `getChecksum`); ready marks move to the content hash; `themes` dropped; `ANALYZE` after the conversion                                                                                                              | `db/migration/core`, `V20261024114500`                               | ✅ `ThemeStorageConversion` both ways over system, demo and an every-field theme (fails when the conversion diverges: checked), `DataPreservationMigrationIT` |
| Guards: `CodeMigrationGuardTest` (imports, pinned checksum), `DroppedContentTableSqlTest` (no SQL on `themes`)                                                                                                                                                                                          | `modules/guards`                                                     | ✅                                                                                                                                                            |

**Measured** (`ThemeStoragePerfTest`, `:modules:epistola-core:perfTest --rerun`; Apple M1 Pro,
Testcontainers Postgres 17 on tmpfs, machine shared with other work; 3 tenants × 40 catalogs × 25
themes with the bundled themes' styles, each catalog released once: 3,000 themes; measured in one
tenant twice, straight after seeding (before the planner has statistics, as on a fresh
installation) and after `ANALYZE`; median / p95 of 200 runs, drafts 40; ms; before = `release/2.0`):

| Operation                                              | before stats: before | after           | with stats: before | after           |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------- | --------------- | ------------------ | --------------- |
| list a catalog's themes (25)                           | 2.06 / 3.04          | 1.33 / 1.59     | 1.04 / 2.18        | 1.23 / 2.00     |
| list a tenant's themes (1,000)                         | 14.03 / 14.77        | 16.05 / 17.06   | 13.56 / 14.49      | 16.79 / 19.83   |
| read one theme                                         | 0.91 / 1.02          | 0.87 / 0.98     | 0.57 / 0.70        | 0.52 / 0.61     |
| save a theme                                           | 4.23 / 5.89          | 9.02 / 15.73    | 2.94 / 3.30        | 5.17 / 8.81     |
| start a draft                                          | 255.55 / 315.54      | 215.07 / 284.67 | 131.11 / 155.19    | 173.09 / 219.46 |
| seeding: create a theme / release a catalog (averages) | 4.5 / 166            | 6.3 / 157       |                    |                 |

**Plans without statistics** (fixed after the first measurement). On a fresh database, releasing a
catalog grew from 0.3 s to 8 s over 25 catalogs and dropped to 0.11 s the moment autoanalyze ran.
`auto_explain` showed the cause: pgjdbc server-prepares a statement after five runs and Postgres
kept the generic plan made on the empty tables, for `ExportThemes` (81 calls, 74 s) and
`ThemeReads.get` (75 calls, 8.7 s): `resource_contents` (estimated one row) outermost, the tenant's
draft rows rescanned once per stored content (667 × 2.6 ms). Fixed without depending on statistics:
the rows of the meant version come from one SQL function, `catalog_version_resources(type)`, whose
shape is fixed (current versions first, then each one's rows through a fenced LATERAL on the draft
key, address columns taken from the version so a caller's tenant and catalog reach it); the theme's
content and its catalog's name are looked up by key (a subquery, the mapper) rather than joined; the
draft key leads with the catalog. Generic plans on the empty tables now take 0.75 ms (a catalog) and
7.3 ms (a tenant). `ThemePlanStabilityTest` releases 20 catalogs on a fresh, never-analyzed database
and requires the last releases to stay within 300 ms or twice the first: it failed before (4.6 s at
the 20th) and passes after. A save is two statements more than before (content and its references)
plus the lock read; dropping the read-back after the update was the cheap win, and it left the rest.

**Noticed, left alone:** `CatalogDraftCommands.holders` reads `release_resources` directly (all
drafts and releases), not through the one read place, since it is cross-version bookkeeping. The
three theme foreign keys point at `catalog_resources`, not `themes`, so none had to go.
`ImportTheme`'s "exists" check is not catalog-scoped (as before).

**Noticed, left alone:** deleting a release or catalog still removes the content only it held at

ADR 0033 says rows go at once and content later, which the collector now makes possible. The
reaper's and the collector's blob passes overlap (both use `BLOB_IS_HELD`). Both done in step 8.

### ADR 0034 section 9, step 1: catalog identity (23 October; branch `refactor/catalog-identity`, not pushed)

A catalog is `CatalogId(namespace, key, tenant)` everywhere; SQL takes the address
(`epistola_catalog_id(tenant, namespace, key)`, `V20261023112019`); the ambient `CatalogNames` scope,
the key-only lookup, the `shared-catalog-keys` toggle and the `epistola_catalog_key_unshared` trigger
are gone. Content keeps `{alias, key}` with alias = key; `CatalogReferences.resolve(from, alias)` is
the one resolver. Each item of phase 4's "still keyed by catalog key" list:

| Item                                                                                                                                                                                | Where                                                                               | Coverage                                                                                        |

| Upstream check worker claims and records by `catalog_id`                                                                                                                            | `CatalogUpstreamCheckStore.kt`                                                      | ✅ `TwoLettersStoredReferencesTest` (upstream)                                                  |
| 2.0 upgrade runner by `CatalogId`                                                                                                                                                   | `CatalogUpgradeRunner.kt`                                                           | ✅ `CatalogUpgradeTest` (+1, two letters)                                                       |
| Content-hash backfill by `CatalogId`                                                                                                                                                | `ReleaseContentHashBackfill.kt`                                                     | ✅ `TwoLettersStoredReferencesTest` (backfill)                                                  |
| Render pins and borrowing by alias through the referencing catalog                                                                                                                  | `ReleaseRenderSource.kt`, `GetCatalogBorrowing.kt`, `DraftDependencyView.kt`        | ✅ `TwoLettersStoredReferencesTest` (render, borrowing)                                         |
| Exchange bindings keyed by the name published (namespace + key)                                                                                                                     | `V20261023112019`, `ExchangeNamespaceBinder.kt`                                     | ✅ `TwoLettersStoredReferencesTest` (bindings)                                                  |
| Stencil usage page by dependency rows; theme usage page by `catalog_id` (it compared theme catalog keys)                                                                            | `StencilReferences.kt`, `GetStencilUsagePage.kt`, `GetThemeUsagePage.kt`            | ✅ `TwoLettersStoredReferencesTest` (stencil, theme usage)                                      |
| Quality findings: URNs name the catalog address; migration rewrites recorded URNs                                                                                                   | `QualityModel.kt`, `V20261023122629` (quality)                                      | ✅ `QualityTwoLettersIntegrationTest`                                                           |
| MCP tenant-wide lists: editability by `CatalogId`; `catalogId` = `namespace/key` or a key one catalog has                                                                           | `McpTenantContext.kt` (`mcpCatalog`), tools                                         | ✅ `McpCatalogToolsIntegrationTest` (+1, +1 rewritten)                                          |
| Editor image URL: `…/catalogs/{ns}/{key}/images/{image}/content?alias=…` from the template's catalog                                                                                | `editor-boot.js`, `AssetHandler.kt` (`ResolveCatalogAlias`)                         | ✅ `TwoLettersPagesHtmxTest` (images)                                                           |
| Organise deep links and plans by address (`type:ns/key/resource`); `ResourceAddress.at` refuses a key alone                                                                         | `CatalogOrganiseHandler.kt`, `CatalogOrganise.ts`, `ResourceGraph.kt`               | ✅ `TwoLettersPagesHtmxTest` (organise), `CatalogOrganiseRoutesTest`, `CatalogOrganise.test.ts` |
| A key that is also a list name: the address router first asks whether the tenant has that catalog                                                                                   | `CatalogScopedRoutes.kt`                                                            | ✅ `TwoLettersPagesHtmxTest` (key `templates`)                                                  |
| Every page links each catalog by its address (lists, graph, home, nav, dialogs, redirects); key alone redirects (GET) or is served (actions) while one catalog has it, else 400/404 | templates, handlers, `Workspace.kt`, `CatalogGraphView.kt`                          | ✅ `TwoLettersPagesHtmxTest`, `CatalogAddressHtmxTest`, app integration suite                   |
| Test lookup of a fixture catalog's namespace bound per test thread (it was one static, read across Spring contexts running concurrently)                                            | `TestCatalogs.kt`                                                                   | ✅ app integration suite (two Exchange tests failed with it shared)                             |

**Decisions within the design:** `CatalogId.segment()` (paths, URNs) includes the namespace; generation
requests, documents and the deployment log keep the name they recorded; an alias a draft does not
declare is read live from the tenant's one catalog with that key; MCP `defaultTheme` stays `key/theme`.

**Noticed, left alone:** MCP and REST list outputs still name a resource's catalog by key
(`TemplateSummaryInfo.catalogId`), which two catalogs with one key share; `ExchangeCatalogInstaller`
checks a collision at the Exchange address while the import lands at the manifest's namespace (the
same in practice); `ValidationCode.CATALOG_KEY_AMBIGUOUS` is no longer raised by the database.

**Decisions:** links are generated namespaced where a key is shared, not always: an address by key
alone keeps working for the tenant's one catalog with that key, and no existing link changes. A page
reached by namespace links its own catalog namespaced (link builder over the request's names); a list of
several catalogs links each shared key explicitly. Key-alone routes are served before namespaced ones,
after every other router.

resource identities: zip paths and entries by key; two `letters` cannot be snapshotted); the upstream

`catalogId` omitted (editability by key); `FromOtherCatalogs` and the dependency editor's options;
the editor's image content URL pattern (key only). Organise deep links from the resource graph name a
shared catalog `namespace/key`, which the organise page does not parse.

| `CatalogNames` (within, frame, apply), `CatalogNamesApplier`; mediator and JDBI-begun transactions write `epistola.catalog_names`; ambiguity translated to `CATALOG_KEY_AMBIGUOUS` (mediator, UI filter) | `CatalogNames.kt`, `SpringMediator.kt`, `SpringAwareTransactionHandler.kt`, `UiExceptionFilter.kt` | ✅ `SideBySideCatalogsTest` (3: create, edit, release, render; ambiguous refused; received beside) |

| Create, register and import name their catalog (import also its declared catalogs); a received catalog of another namespace is installed beside                                                          | `CreateCatalog.kt`, `RegisterCatalog.kt`, `ImportCatalogZip.kt`, `RestoreTenantSnapshot.kt`        | ✅ `CatalogBundleTest`, `CatalogIntegrationTest` (both rewritten from "refused" to "beside")       |
| UI addresses `/catalogs/{namespace}/{key}/…`, links rewritten, by-key page GETs redirected while unambiguous; catalog list links namespaced; a new catalog lands on its namespaced page                  | `CatalogAddressFilter.kt`, `catalogs/list.html`, `CatalogHandler.kt`                               | ✅ `CatalogAddressHtmxTest` (2)                                                                    |

| Test principal filter ordered as declared (-98), so filters after it see the principal                                                                                                                   | `TestSecurityContextConfiguration.kt`                                                              | ✅ app suite                                                                                       |

**Decisions:** resolution in the database (one function, read by every lookup) rather than threading a
namespace through every command; the namespace travels as transaction-local names; ambiguity is a
refusal, never a pick. UI addresses are served by the existing by-key routes behind a filter, so no
route or template changed beyond the catalog list.

**Noticed, left alone:** aliases other than the key (`CATALOG_DEPENDENCY_ALIAS_UNSUPPORTED` kept;
references, render, merge and validation resolve another catalog by key); tenant-wide maps keyed by
catalog key (`CatalogEditabilities`, nav, resource graph, `IN (<keys>)` lookups) collapse two catalogs
with one key; the nav's catalog links and other templates outside the catalog list still link by key
(redirected, or refused when ambiguous); REST unchanged (its paths name the namespace already, but its
handlers look up by key without naming it: ambiguous keys are refused over REST); the demo app on port
4000 was stopped by a `gradlew --stop` during this work.

| Demo: the demo tenant is created with, and on every start granted, `exchange.epistola.app/demo`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `DemoLoader.kt`                                                                                                                                           | ✅ `DemoTenantListReachableIT` (+1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Fixture tenants (`createTenant`, `fixture`, `scenario`) are created holding their key's namespace under the default registry (`TestNamespaces`), the names catalogs had before; tests that named other namespaces, received and released, or created tenants directly, grant what they need                                                                 | `TestNamespaces.kt`, ~60 test files                                                                                                                       | ✅ the suites below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

**Decisions where the task left room:**

- **Existing data**: a tenant authors a catalog that has no source (so not supplied by Epistola, not
  installed from the Exchange, a registry or a manifest URL), was not a 1.x subscribed copy
  (`catalogs.type`), and was not received as a file (a release naming another installation, and none
  cut here). A release recorded before releases named their installation counts as cut here: every
  such catalog could be released until now, so being generous loses nobody a release. Exchange
  grants use `exchange.epistola.app`, as the 2.0 identity migration did.
- **`local`**: a tenant may name catalogs under `local` with any namespace it picks, without a grant,
  except a token it holds under another authority (ADR 0034, section 2). The contract does not carry
  `local` (catalog format and API registry rule want a dot; contract ADR 0003 defers it), so a local
  catalog is released and deployed here and refused wherever it would become an archive. The
  automatic default for a tenant created without a namespace is `local/default`, not the tenant key.
- **Revoke**: refused while catalogs the tenant authors (no source) are named in it, naming them; a
  received catalog does not block it. Exchange grants are never removed automatically (a withdrawn
  namespace or a disconnect): the catalogs named in them keep releasing here; publishing still
  checks the connection.
- **UI place**: a platform page reached from the tenant list, not the tenant's Settings, because
  granting is a platform administrator's (`TENANT_MANAGER`), who need not be a member; the tenant's
  own people see what it holds in the create dialog and MCP.
- **Choice control**: radios rather than a select, so the `local` namespace field shows with the
  existing `data-radio-panes` behaviour; preselected when one is held.
- **Tenant creation**: the tenant form requires a namespace (authority defaulted), so a tenant made
  in the UI never starts with a `local` default catalog.
- **REST**: the contract has no create operation; releasing over REST refuses with 400
  `catalog-namespace-not-held`. Nothing else in `rest-api` changed.

**Runs** (`--no-watch-fs`, final code but for the local refusal's wording, after which
`CatalogNamingTest` ran again, 7 green): core integration 1309 (1 skipped, 0 failed), app
integration 799, demo integration 39, MCP integration 97, rest-api 35, guards 28, `ktlintCheck`,
`checkMigrationVersions` and `checkChangelogFragments` (base `refactor/catalog-releases`) green.
Each rule was seen failing its test with the rule taken out (15 mutations: release grant, local
release validation, local export, Exchange local, revoke in use, Exchange grants, create held,
change held, the migration's source and file rules, the readiness block, the dialog's choice, the
tenant form's namespace, MCP's namespace, the demo grant). `uiTest` not run.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- **Contract follow-up (blocking `local`)**: `CatalogNamespaces.REGISTRY_PATTERN` and the API's
  `Registry` pattern refuse `local`. Until the contract accepts it, a local catalog cannot be
  exported, sent to a container registry, kept in a tenant snapshot (so `BuildTenantSnapshot` fails
  for a tenant that has one, such as a demo personal tenant's `local/default/default`), copied into a
  draft from one of its releases (`CatalogReleaseCopier` goes through an archive: parallel work from
  a release, reopening the next version), or addressed over REST.
- **Contract follow-up**: namespace grants are not in the API (ADR 0034 consequences), and there is
  no create-catalog operation that would name a namespace.
- Receiving still derives a namespace from the tenant key for an archive from before wire v8:
  `ImportCatalogZip` (`manifest.catalog.namespace ?: defaultNamespace(tenant)`), `RegisterCatalog`,
  `AvailableCatalogReleases`, REST `EpistolaCatalogApi` (import), `EpistolaTenantApi`, and the import
  dialog's proposal (`CatalogImportHandler`). Receiving needs no grant, so it was left.
- `ChangeCatalogNamespace` keeps the authority: a catalog cannot move from `local` into a held
  namespace (promotion is phase 8), so a tenant's `local/default/default` stays local.
- A non-local catalog depending on a local one releases here, but its export names a local
  dependency, which the contract's validator refuses with its own words.
- ADR 0034, section 7's example `local/hr/letters` is not a valid name: a namespace has at least 3
  characters.
- Playwright UI tests (`uiTest`) not run; their direct `CreateTenant` calls now go through
  `TestNamespaces`, and `CatalogDialogUiTest` drives the create dialog, whose namespace choice is
  preselected.
- The tenant's own administrators have no read-only view of the namespaces it holds beyond the create
  dialog and MCP.
