touched").

**Decisions where the task left room:**

- The no-change refusal is opt-in on the command (`requireChange`), set by REST and MCP only. Applied to
  every caller, it failed 29 core tests that release unchanged drafts on purpose (deployments, served releases, the
  same-content listing, numbering), and the web UI keeps its notes-only re-release; the contract
  speaks for the API. Say if the UI should refuse too.
- A release whose recorded fingerprint is V5 shows its backfilled `content_hash` (null until the
  backfill reaches it); `contentHash` is never the V5 fingerprint.
- The filter is answered by the database: `ListReleasesByContentHash` filters on
  `catalog_releases.content_hash` (`idx_catalog_releases_content_hash`), orders by
  `releaseNumberOrder` and pages with `LIMIT`/`OFFSET` and a windowed count; a release whose hash is
  not computed yet is no match, a `status=draft` filter an empty page. (First built as an in-memory
  filter over the listing; moved the same day.) ✅ `ListReleasesByContentHashTest` (3: matches only,
  highest first, deployments; paging with the count; pending is no match), each failing with its
  part of the SQL removed; `ContentAddressedReleasesApiIT` pages the filter over HTTP. The unfiltered
  listing still pages in memory (SemVer order), as before.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- MCP `update_catalog_dependencies` takes no alias (REST does); here it could only be the key.
- MCP `AttributeInfo.codeListBinding.catalog` names the code list's catalog key, also for the own
  catalog, where REST now leaves it out.
- `docs/code-lists.md` still lists "variant attribute references become catalog-qualified" as a
  follow-up; the REST half (`VariantSelectionAttribute.catalogKey`) is done, the validator half not
  checked.

### Which releases a draft lacks, drawn (22 October; branch `refactor/catalog-releases`, not pushed)

Reported in the demo (`test`/`default`, next release on 1.2.1, lacking 1.0.1 and 1.1.1): both pages
printed one sentence per release ("v1.0.1 was published in between and is not in the next release
…"), and two of them read as a puzzle ("why two?"). The catalog's page and the draft's page each had
their own copy of that block, which had already drifted once (Merge… only on one).

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                                                                                                           | Coverage                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ReleaseLineage.behindLine` → `BehindLine` (`line`: first parents from the oldest release a lacking one hangs off up to the base; `joined`: merge parents on that stretch and the draft's own merges, `JoinedRelease(version, into)`; `behind`: `BehindBranch(version, madeOn)`, null when made on nothing drawn here)                                                               | `releases/ReleaseLineage.kt`, `releases/BehindLine.kt`                                                          | ✅ `BehindLineTest` (7, unit: demo shape, one missing, merged into the line, merged into the draft, received from elsewhere / no base, a chain of missing ones, from nothing)                                                                           |
| `BehindRelease.base` (as its line names it) and `releasedAt`; `ReleasedChange.name` (the release's name for it, a removed one by its base's); `CatalogRelease.behindLine`, filled with `behind`                                                                                                                                                                                      | `releases/CatalogReleases.kt`, `releases/ListCatalogReleases.kt`                                                | ✅ `ReleaseMergeParentsTest` +2 (the demo's shape through `GetCatalogDraft(withBehind)`, and with 1.0.1 merged into 1.2.0); `ReleaseBaseTest`, `SeveralDraftsTest` now assert the names                                                                 |
| One fragment for both pages, `catalogs/behind.html` (testids kept): one release a sentence ("v1.0.1 was made on v1.0.0 and is not in the next release: it changed Brand 2."); several a group: title with the count, why, a server-drawn SVG (`role="img"`, `aria-label`), a row each (version linked to its page, made on, published, what changed, Merge…), the withheld hint once | `catalogs/behind.html`, `overview.html`, `next-release.html`, `CatalogBehindView.kt`, `CatalogDraftBar.missing` | ✅ `CatalogBehindHtmxTest` (3, both pages each: one missing, two missing, a viewer without the content author role); all 3 failed before. `CatalogBehindViewTest` (6, unit: wording, layout without overlaps, joined above, folding, unattached dashed) |
| Measuring what a draft is behind reads each release's content on the handle it already holds (`ReleaseContentAssembler.assemble(handle, …)`), not a second connection: with the new pages, concurrent page loads held one connection each and waited for a second, and the test pool (8) ran dry                                                                                     | `revisions/ReleaseContentAssembler.kt`, `releases/ListCatalogReleases.kt`                                       | ✅ the app set below (26 classes, 173 tests) failed twice with 7 `HikariPool … Connection is not available` (in tenant setup) before, passed twice after; without `CatalogBehindHtmxTest` it passed before too                                          |
| Docs (`catalog-versioning.md`, Behind), changelog (merge fragment, one sentence)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | docs, changelog                                                                                                 | ➖                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

**Decisions where the task left room:**

- **The "why" line** does not say "after it started": in the demo 1.0.1 was published before 1.2.0,
  and behind is by lineage, not time. It says "They were published from separate work on earlier
  releases and never taken into the next release."
- **Where it was made:** "made on v1.0.0"; for a base this catalog does not have "received from
  elsewhere, made on v0.9.0"; for none "started from nothing", as the Releases table says.
- **The drawing:** the line in a row ending in the draft; lacking releases below, off the release
  they were made on (dashed amber edge, amber box, "not in it"); merged-in releases above, joined
  where they were taken in ("merged in"); a lacking release made on nothing drawn here is a dashed box
  without an edge; three or more line releases in a row with nothing off them fold into "n more". A
  merged-in release's own base is not connected: the extra edge shared a vertical with another join
  and read as a T-junction. Captions carry a halo so an edge through them stays readable.
- **Names:** what a release changed is said by name (from its content), grouped as changed, added
  and removed, at most five names per kind.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- The overview's next-release box says "Everything is published, as v1.1.1." when the highest
  release is a fix from other work the next release lacks (`CatalogDraftBar.latestPublished` is the
  highest release, not the next release's base). Seen in `CatalogBehindHtmxTest`'s catalog; in the
  demo the highest release is the base, so it does not show there.
- MCP `list_catalog_releases` `behind` and REST `BehindReleaseDto` still carry only the version and
  changes; the base and publication time are web UI only (REST needs a contract change).
- Running `:apps:epistola:integrationTest` rebuilds `apps/epistola/build/libs/epistola-dev-plain.jar`,
  which a demo started with `bootRun` from the same worktree reads: its static files answered 500
  afterwards until restarted.

### Catalog-level changes are changes of the next release (22 October; branch `feat/release-visibility`, not pushed)

Reported in the demo: `default` (everything published) declared `system` (locked to 2.0.1), and its
draft page still said "Everything is published. What you change from now on shows up here.", so
the dependency could not be published. `GetCatalogResourceChanges`, which the draft page, the
publish dialog, the bar, the overview and `ReleaseCatalogDraft` all ask "has the draft changed?",
compared resources only.

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Where                                                                                                        | Coverage                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `CatalogResourceChanges.settings` (`CatalogSettingChange`: entry, state, name, dependency `lock`/`baseLock`) by `CatalogSettingDiff`, against the base's manifest (`ReleaseContentAssembler.readManifest`): dependencies by alias and pin (number and fingerprint; a named draft as its base; not registry/namespace), default theme, presets, license; presentation not; `hasUnreleasedChanges` counts them                           | `catalog/queries/CatalogSettingChange.kt`, `GetCatalogResourceChanges.kt`, `ReleaseContentAssembler.kt`      | ✅ `CatalogSettingChangesTest` (10: dependency added/removed/re-pinned, default theme, presets, license changed; presentation-only unchanged; dependency-only draft publishes with another content hash; a named draft compared as its base); 8 of the first 9 failed before, the presentation guard passed before as it must; the named-draft case fails with the strict pin rule (mutation) |
| A dependency's named draft whose only change is a catalog setting has something to publish: published along (`GetCatalogBorrowing.unpublished`), and `ReleaseCatalogDraft.unchangedBase` releases it instead of pinning its base                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `GetCatalogBorrowing.kt`                                                                                     | ✅ `CatalogSettingChangesTest` along case (failed before: pinned `1.1.0`, the base)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Number rules: preset removed breaking, added additive, renamed a fix; default theme and license a fix (reasons at `NONE`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `releases/ChangeKind.kt`                                                                                     | ✅ `ChangeKindEvaluatorTest` +2 (both failed before), `CatalogSettingChangesTest` (presets minor then major, default theme patch)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Draft page rows and publish dialog: "Added: Shared (Dependency) — locked to v1.0.0", "Changed: … — now locked to v1.1.0 (was v1.0.0)", "Removed: … — was locked to v1.0.0", "… — its draft Next release, published along", "Default theme / Presets / License (Catalog setting)"; counts on the bar and overview include them (`changeCount`); `typeLabel("catalogDependency")` is "Dependency" (merge dialog too, was "Catalog used") | `CatalogReleaseReadiness`, `next-release.html`, `list.html`, `overview.html`, `workspace.html`, `merge.html` | ✅ `CatalogNextReleaseHandlerHtmxTest` +1 (failed before: the page showed `catalog-no-changes`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| MCP `get_catalog_changes` returns `settings` (`entry`, `state`, `name`, `releaseVersion`, `draftName`, `baseReleaseVersion`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `mcp/dto/CatalogChangesInfo.kt`, `ReadyMcpTools`, `docs/mcp.md`                                              | ✅ `McpReadyToolsIntegrationTest` +1 (fails with the mapping removed, checked by mutation)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Dependencies page ("Depends on"): a "Named in content as" column, same header and title as the draft's table (`BorrowedCatalog.alias`: the declaration's alias, else the key the references name)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `GetCatalogBorrowing.kt`, `catalogs/content.html`                                                            | ✅ `CatalogWorkspacePagesHtmxTest` +1 (failed before)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Docs: catalog-level changes, ready marks, number rules, the Dependencies page (`catalog-versioning.md`), `settings` (`mcp.md`); changelog fragment `20261022124839-catalog-setting-changes.md`, alias entry extended                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | docs, changelog                                                                                              | ➖                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**Decisions where the task left room:**

- **Ready marks:** a catalog setting never needs one and never blocks a release. `ReadyMarks`
  already said settings carry no marks; a mark records a resource's content digest, and choosing a
  dependency, default theme, presets or license is one deliberate act with nothing half-done to
  review. So a draft whose only changes are settings publishes as it is.
- **Numbers:** dependency rules unchanged (added minor, removed or moved-and-fits patch, moved-and-
  breaks-what-is-used major). Presets follow the resource rule, since other catalogs' content may
  name them (ADR 0007): removed major, added minor, renamed patch. Default theme and license are a
  patch: a theme's own content changing is a fix, and the default theme only decides which theme
  this catalog's templates that name none render with; nothing another catalog or a caller relies on
  changes. (The task guessed "at least a minor" for the default theme; one line in
  `ChangeKindEvaluator.settings` changes it.)
- **"Same pin":** number and fingerprint; registry and namespace are left out (a rename or mirror
  is no change, ADR 0034). A declaration naming a draft is compared as the release it pins when
  that draft has nothing to publish, its base, as `ReleaseCatalogDraft` pins it; what that draft
  changed itself stays the other catalog's change, published along (`onlyOthersChanged`). The
  strict rule (a named draft differs from any pin) broke `PublishDialogHtmxTest`'s "a change only in
  the catalog it uses" case, which is how this was found. A move between two numbers with the same
  fingerprint (a notes-only re-release) counts as a change although the content hash would not
  differ.
- Settings are a separate list, not entries in `resources`: REST maps `resources` to
  `ResourceChangeDto`, whose `type` is a resource type.
- Before a first release every setting the draft has is listed as added.

**Runs** (`--no-watch-fs`): before the fix, `CatalogSettingChangesTest` 8 of 9 failed,
`ChangeKindEvaluatorTest` 2 of 8, `CatalogNextReleaseHandlerHtmxTest` and
`CatalogWorkspacePagesHtmxTest` 1 each (the new cases); the MCP case fails with its mapping
removed, the named-draft case with the strict pin rule. After: core `--tests
"app.epistola.suite.catalog.*"` 545, core unit 1033, app integration 777 (775 green,
`DataPreservationMigrationIT` ×2 known: `UpgradePreflightRefused`), of which `*Catalog*` 192 and the
`catalog` package 160, `:modules:epistola-mcp:integrationTest` 94, `:modules:guards:test` 28,
`ktlintCheck` green. The running demo was not restarted, so it still shows the old page.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- **REST (contract):** `getCatalogChanges` lists resources only; `dependenciesChanged` covers
  dependencies, the default theme, presets and license have no field. Its `releasable` is
  `notReady.isEmpty()`, while the contract says "… and something changed".
- `NextVersionChanges` (fast-forward on receiving) compares dependencies by their full declaration,
  registry and namespace included, and not the license, which is catalog-wide here: a received
  release overwrites a license edited in the next version without it counting as a change of its own.
- What a behind release changed (`ListCatalogReleases.changesOf`, "it changed …") lists resources only.
- A setting resolved with merged content still gets no "Merged, review before publishing" badge (no
  mark to end it).
- A received release that carries `compatibility` hashes it; a draft built here has none, which no
  change lists.
- The same release pinned under two fingerprint algorithms (V5 in the base, the content hash after a
  re-pin) is listed as "now locked to v2.0.0 (was v2.0.0)".

### ADR 0034 phase 2 made visible (22 October; branch `feat/release-visibility` from `ff5e0dd68`, not pushed)

What phase 2 records, shown to people: a release's content hash and the releases it is the same as
on the release page, a dependency's alias on the draft's page, and the content hash over MCP.

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Where                                                                                  | Coverage                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ListReleasesWithSameContent` (`CATALOG_VIEW`): the other releases of the tenant with the same content hash, any catalog, any number, the catalog's own first (highest first), then other catalogs by name; reads only what is recorded, so a release without its hash is `pending` and lists none, and is not listed for others                                                                               | `catalog/queries/ListReleasesWithSameContent.kt`                                       | ✅ `ListReleasesWithSameContentTest` (4: notes-only re-release both ways and a changed one not; another catalog after the own, another tenant not; pending, found after the backfill; unknown release null). The tenant test failed with the tenant filter removed (mutation) |
| The release detail carries `contentHash`, `shortContentHash` and `recordedBeforeContentHashes` (its fingerprint is not V6)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `GetCatalogReleaseDetail.kt`                                                           | ✅ `CatalogReleaseReadQueriesTest` (+1: cut now, recorded before, given by the backfill)                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Release page: "Content fingerprint" (short, whole in the title, copy button via `data-copy-source`), "Being computed" while null; "Original fingerprint" with "This release was recorded before content fingerprints and has been given one." (or "; it is being given one."); "The same release as" with links (`v1.0.0`, `Forms v1.0.0`) and "The same content, under another number or in another catalog." | `CatalogHandler.releaseDetail` (`sameAs`), `catalogs/release.html`                     | ✅ `CatalogReleaseIdentityHtmxTest` (2; both failed against the previous template)                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Draft page, Dependencies under Advanced: a "Named in content as" column with each declaration's alias                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `catalogs/next-release.html`                                                           | ✅ `CatalogNextReleaseHandlerHtmxTest` (+1; failed against the previous template)                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| MCP `list_catalog_releases` returns `contentHash` per release (null for a draft and while being computed); `CatalogRelease.contentHash` read by `CatalogReleaseReader`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `CatalogReleaseInfo.kt`, `CatalogReleases.kt`, `ListCatalogReleases.kt`, `docs/mcp.md` | ✅ `CatalogReleaseMcpToolsIntegrationTest` (+1, on the result as JSON; failed with the mapping removed)                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Test helper `recordBeforeContentHashes`: plays a release recorded before content hashes (V5 fingerprint, no content hash) by SQL, since no command produces that state                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `modules/testing/.../TestReleaseIdentity.kt`                                           | ➖ used by the tests above                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Docs: the release page and the alias column (`catalog-versioning.md`), `contentHash` (`mcp.md`); changelog fragment `20261022115928-release-content-fingerprint.md`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | docs, changelog                                                                        | ➖                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**Decisions where the task left room:**

- The Releases list (`catalogs/overview.html :: versions`) gets no content hash on hover: a title
  is invisible on touch and to keyboard users, a hash string means nothing to an author scanning
  numbers, and the release page, one click away, shows it with a copy button and the releases it is
  the same as.
- "The same release as" stays inside the tenant, as asked, and reads only recorded hashes: the page
  is a GET and computes nothing; the backfill fills the gaps.
- The recorded fingerprint is shown only when it is of an older kind than the content hash (a V6
  release's fingerprint is its content hash, so one value), under "Original fingerprint".
- Demo catalog: nothing added. Every release page of the demo tenant shows its content
  fingerprint; "The same release as" needs two releases with the same content in one tenant, which a
  bundled catalog (one release per number, cut elsewhere) cannot stage.
- REST: unchanged; the contract's release DTOs have no content hash field (follow-up below).

**Runs** (`--no-watch-fs`): the new tests (`ListReleasesWithSameContentTest` 4, `CatalogReleaseReadQueriesTest` 6 with
the new one, `CatalogReleaseIdentityHtmxTest` 2, `CatalogNextReleaseHandlerHtmxTest` 10 with the new
one) green; the three new handler tests failed against the previous templates, the MCP test with
the mapping removed, the same-content tests with the tenant filter removed. Core
`--tests "app.epistola.suite.catalog.*"` 535, app `--tests "*Catalog*"` 190,
`:modules:epistola-mcp:integrationTest` 93, `:modules:guards:test` 28, `ktlintCheck`, all green
(0 failed, 0 skipped).

**Noticed, left alone:**

- **API follow-up (contract):** REST release DTOs (`CatalogVersion` and the release list) have no
  content hash; add `contentHash` beside `fingerprint`, and later address a release by it (ADR 0034
  "Consequences, Contract (API)").
- `idx_catalog_releases_content_hash` is `(tenant_key, catalog_id, content_hash)`; the tenant-wide
  lookup uses only its tenant prefix. A `(tenant_key, content_hash)` index if tenants hold many
  releases.
- Releases with no content in two catalogs share a content hash, so they are "the same release";
  true by the model, and possibly surprising.
- The release page does not list the release's own dependencies (what it pinned, under which alias),
  although its content hash covers them.
- The Dependencies page's "Depends on" table (`catalogs/content.html :: borrowed`) shows no alias: it
  names catalogs by display name and lists catalogs used but not declared.
- MCP `release_draft` returns `fingerprint` (V6, so the content hash, for a release cut now) and no
  `contentHash`; `list_catalogs`' `fingerprint` is the highest release's recorded one, possibly V5.
- The copy button's fallback without the Clipboard API (plain HTTP off localhost) cannot select a
  hidden input and says "Copy failed — select the value and copy it manually"; the whole value is
  then only in the title.
- `ReleaseIdentityTest` keeps its own copy of the V5 rewrite that `recordBeforeContentHashes` now
  shares.
- The Releases list does not mark a release published again with only new notes.

### Release integrity: a release holds what its record says (22 October; branch `refactor/catalog-releases`, not pushed)

Started from the local demo database: tenant `demo`'s `system 2.0.0` kept all nine fonts with
`"variants": []`, tenant `test`'s the same release with four faces each, both with the same entry
fingerprints (inter `13118e23…`) and V5 fingerprint, but different backfilled content hashes
(`v6:d0dbde43…`, `v6:abb18ecf…`).

**Cause of the faceless fonts.** The database was created from empty: all 72 migrations ran in one
second (19 October 20:13:41 UTC), and tenant `demo` was created at 20:13:46.323 UTC in the same
transaction as its `system 2.0.0` release (`released_at` equals `tenants.created_at`). The build then
(between `7dd085581` and `b4130757e`) had `RecordInstalledRelease` retain `contentBuilder.build`
content, whose fonts come from `ExportFonts`, which keeps only `source = 'ASSET'` faces. Fixed by
`b4130757e` (20 October 08:05 +0200, findings G4), before tenant `test` was created (10:26 +0200). No
migration writes `resource_revisions` or `release_entries`. None of the 2.0 migrations is on `main` or
in a tag (`main` ends at `V20260923150918`, 1.5.0), so no installation has such rows: no repair. The
two backfilled hashes are faithful: `v6:d0dbde43…` is the hash of the faceless content, `v6:abb18ecf…`
of the complete one.

**What the current code and its guard still got wrong**, each found by checking the invariant, each fixed with a test
seen failing without the fix:

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Where                                                                     | Coverage                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `DataPreservationMigrationIT` reaches 2.0 the supported way: RC1 data migrated to 1.5.0 (2.0 checks skipped for that step, as 1.x had none), then the 2.0 step with its preflight; asserts every 1.x check ran on the seeded data and found nothing (`ddb1d7149`)                                                                             | `DataPreservationMigrationIT.kt`                                          | ✅ ×2 (PostgreSQL 17, 18); failed ×2 before (`UpgradePreflightRefused`, `tv.template_resource_id`)                                                                                                                                    |
| An installed release is fingerprinted over what it keeps: entry fingerprints, and a fingerprint or content hash computed while recording, were over the content before the bundled faces were added (every system release; a 1.x system release got `v6:d0dbde43…` while keeping `v6:abb18ecf…`) (`290565498`)                                | `RecordInstalledRelease.kt`                                               | ✅ `InstalledReleaseRecordTest` (2), both failing without                                                                                                                                                                             |
| The 1.x path end to end: a 1.5.0-shaped database with 1.x data, the app starting on it, the upgrade runner and the backfill; every release keeps its fonts' faces and its theme's spacing unit, records entries with the fingerprint of their revision, and reproduces its content hash and fingerprint (system: see decisions) (`d3dd4ecf6`) | `OneXUpgradeReleasesIT.kt`                                                | ✅ 1; failed before `290565498` on the nine system font entries only (no face lost on the 1.x path), and before `e1c1f3ab8` on the spacing unit; with retention without bundled faces (the code before `b4130757e`) it fails on faces |
| No content hash for retained content that no longer reproduces the fingerprint the release was recorded with; the backfill says how many were left. Before, such content was hashed anyway, giving one release two identities (the demo's `system 2.0.0`) and letting `travelling` export it (`6afb8a5df`)                                    | `ReleaseIdentity.kt`, `ReleaseContentHashBackfill.kt`                     | ✅ `ReleaseIdentityTest` +1 (without: `v6:39e128b2…` recorded, and the export went through); the existing V5 backfill and export tests still pass                                                                                     |
| A theme's spacing unit is exported, released and imported: `ExportThemes` read the NUMERIC column `as? Float`, always null, since 1.0.0 (`b525d28a1`); releases rendered with the default 4pt (`e1c1f3ab8`, fragment `20261022122747-theme-spacing-unit.md`)                                                                                  | `ExportResources.kt`                                                      | ✅ `ReleaseRenderSourceTest` +1 (4.0 instead of 8.0), `CatalogThemeSettingsTest` +1 (null instead of 8.0), `OneXUpgradeReleasesIT`                                                                                                    |
| An installed catalog's theme settings are not changes of its own: the baseline was recorded before the archive's default theme and presets were written, so a catalog installed from a source with theme settings refused every upgrade (`318b96c10`)                                                                                         | `RecordInstalledRelease.kt`                                               | ✅ `DemoCatalogUpgradeReleaseIntegrationTest` (both settings reported as changes without)                                                                                                                                             |
| A release brought in by an upgrade keeps the archive it came in (`InstalledContentHandoff`), as a fresh install does; rebuilt from the installed rows it hashed to `v6:e7324a50…` while named `v6:fc33bbcd…` and could not be exported (`375f002f6`)                                                                                          | `UpgradeCatalog.kt`, `InstallFromCatalog.kt`, `RecordInstalledRelease.kt` | ✅ `DemoCatalogUpgradeReleaseIntegrationTest` (failed without: the hash, and with that assertion removed the export's refusal)                                                                                                        |

**Decisions where the task left room:**

- The preflight is right to read the 1.2.0+ shape: direct upgrades are promised from supported versions
  only (`SUPPORT_POLICY.md`), and 1.4.0 and 1.5.0 both end on `V20260923150918`. So the fixture takes the
  supported route rather than the checks learning the RC1 shape. Every migration after RC1 still runs on
  the seeded data and every assertion is unchanged.
- The system catalog's content hash stays the shipped archive's (findings G6): dependents pin it, and its
  release also holds the seeded fonts and is rebuilt from the installed rows, so it never reproduces.
  `OneXUpgradeReleasesIT` asserts that, plus its faces and entries. ADR 0032 section 8 (fonts in the
  system catalog's archive) is the real fix.
- Refuse rather than flag: a content hash that names other content than the release is worse than none.
  This reverses, for releases still without a hash, phase 2's choice to hash releases with dropped
  qualifiers "as read now". None is left in the demo database: all 18 releases had their hash.
- No assert at record time: after the fixes above the one value is fingerprinted, hashed and retained;
  reading it back would need the uncommitted transaction, the storage round trip is
  `ReleaseRoundTripTest`'s, and for the system catalog it would always fail.
- Changelog: only the spacing unit shipped (1.x exports); the rest never did.

**The demo database** (copy, booted with `6afb8a5df`; backfill hashed nothing, nothing failed): only
`demo/system@2.0.0` has faceless fonts; both `system@2.0.1` record their nine font entries faceless
(fixed for new recordings); `epistola-demo@6.4.1` does not reproduce its content hash (the upgrade
rebuild, and its theme's lost spacing unit). Existing rows are left as they are: development data.

**Full runs** (`--no-watch-fs`, final code): core integration 1269 (1 skipped, 0 failed), core unit 1031,
app integration 773 (`DataPreservationMigrationIT` ×2 and `OneXUpgradeReleasesIT` green), demo
integration 38 and unit 45, MCP integration 92, rest-api 34, guards 28 (`DriftRatchetTest` failed once
when run beside the app suite, a file in `build/test-results` vanishing mid-walk; green alone),
`ktlintCheck` green.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- The demo app on port 4000 now runs from worktree `release-visibility` (restarted 14:13:24).
- The spacing unit is lost on `main` too (1.x exports since 1.0.0): whether to fix it in 1.5.x is open.
- Entry fingerprints of older releases on this branch do not reproduce even where V5 does
  (`epistola-demo@6.2.0`: 15 entries; `demo/system@2.0.0`: 8 non-font entries). Not investigated: nothing
  reads them, and the per-resource form is the contract's "current", unnamed, so its meaning can move.
- On a 1.0.x or 1.1.x database (unsupported) the 1.x checks say "could not run, skip it", not "upgrade
  to 1.x first".
- `EnsureSubscribedCatalog`'s already-current path still records from the rebuild (no archive at hand);
  it only records catalogs installed before releases were recorded.
- `changelog/unreleased/20261020080501-release-bundled-fonts.md` describes a fix of behaviour that never
  shipped, which `changelog/README.md` says to delete.

### ADR 0034 phase 2 (22 October; branch `refactor/catalog-releases`, not pushed; contract `2.0.0-SNAPSHOT` at `15de68a`)

Caught up with the content-addressed catalog contract (`18813c5` aliases, `d4f4791` V6, `56a7f80` patch
numbers, `6884498` V6 without the wire version, `15de68a` 128 characters) and identified releases by
their content hash.

| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| `SemVer` reads `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH-<namespace>.<N>` and orders it as SemVer (above its predecessor, below its core, labels ASCII then number); bumping it bumps its core; SQL orders through `releaseNumberOrder`                                                                                                                                                 | `SemVer.kt`, `GetLatestCatalogRelease.kt` and six queries                 | ✅ `SemVerTest` (+4), `PatchReleaseTest`                              |
| A patch release satisfies a range by precedence, as a release (`^1.3.0` takes it, `1.3.1` does not)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `CatalogReleaseSelector.kt`                                               | ✅ `CatalogReleaseSelectorTest` (+8)                                  |

| Releasing here refuses a patch number (phase 5); merge parents up to 128 characters                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `AssessDraftNumber.kt`, `ReleaseCatalogDraft.kt`, `ReleaseDescription.kt` | ✅ `PatchReleaseTest`                                                 |

**Decisions where the task left room:**

- Aliases equal keys in this installation, and an archive with another alias is refused: stored
  references name tenant catalog keys (unique per tenant until phase 4), and rewriting a received
  archive's aliases would change its content hash, so it would no longer be the release it says it
  is. Translating aliases at every boundary (import, render, merge, export) is phase 4's.
- The content hash is a nullable column plus an idempotent cluster task and on-demand computation,
  not a Kotlin Flyway migration (reasons above). Until a release has it, whatever asks computes and
  records it.
- Bundled catalogs moved with slice 1: the contract refuses their V5 fingerprints and alias-less
  declarations, so nothing booted until they did. Versions bumped (system 2.0.0 → 2.0.1, demo 6.4.0 →
  6.4.1), so existing installations receive them as new releases beside the old.
- A patch release matches ranges by precedence, unlike an npm pre-release. (Superseded the same day:
  the contract's `versionRange` now says npm semantics, see "Contract API follow-ups of ADR 0034".)

**Full runs** (`--no-watch-fs`, after slice 3): core integration 1264 (1 skipped, 0 failed), core unit
1031 (`BundledCatalogFingerprintTest` 2 among them), app integration 771 (769 green,
`DataPreservationMigrationIT` ×2 for the known reason), MCP integration 91 (92 with the alias test below), rest-api 34, guards 28,
demo unit 45 (`DemoCatalogFingerprintTest` 1), editor `pnpm test` 2228, `pnpm build`, `ktlintCheck`,
`checkMigrationVersions` and `checkChangelogFragments` (base `release/2.0`) green. The four new
migrations also ran on a copy of the running demo's database (15 releases, all V5): applied, aliases
backfilled; the content hashes are left to the startup task.

Follow-up (same day): the REST released binding and the MCP `alias` got tests of their own
(`ReleasedAttributeBindingApiIT` 1, over HTTP; `CatalogDependencyMcpToolsIntegrationTest` +1), each
seen failing with its fix reverted. Runs: those two classes, `:modules:rest-api:test` 34,
`:modules:epistola-mcp:integrationTest` 92, `ktlintCheck` green.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- Two catalogs cannot share a key in a tenant yet, so an alias other than the key is refused rather
  than stored (phase 4).
- REST `ReleaseVersion` and `CatalogReleaseRef` are core-only in the contract: a patch release cannot
  be named in a REST path or request; REST and MCP release DTOs show the recorded fingerprint, not the
  content hash; the UI shows no content hash either. (UI and MCP since "phase 2 made visible"; REST
  since "Contract API follow-ups of ADR 0034".)
- "The most recently received presentation wins" (contract): a release recorded beside the others
  (not fast-forwarded) does not update the catalog's name, description or metadata.
- Releases retained on this branch between 21 and 22 October with qualified references into another
  namespace no longer reproduce their V5 fingerprint (the contract's model drops typed qualifiers
  when binding); their content hash is over the content as read now, and exports check against
  that. A next version whose baseline was recorded with qualifiers shows those resources changed
  once.
- Exchange publication outbox entries queued before this change hold archives with V5 fingerprints
  (and possibly qualifiers), which a receiver on the new contract refuses: requeue them.
- A tenant snapshot restore records each release with its archive's fingerprint (V6 now), not the
  V5 its `releases[].fingerprint` lists; the content hash is the same.
- `SemVer` numbers are `Int`s: a number beyond that range (possible within 128 characters) is not
  parsed and sorts as a pre-2.0 label.
- A notes-only re-release shares its content hash with the release before it.
- Bare-slug variant attributes (`locale`) still resolve tenant-wide here, while on the wire a key
  without qualifier means the catalog's own.
- The API's own references (`CatalogThemeReference`, an attribute's code-list binding) still name
  registry and namespace (contract unchanged). (Done: "Contract API follow-ups of ADR 0034".)
- ~~`DataPreservationMigrationIT` ×2 still fails for the pre-existing reason~~ (fixed: see "Release integrity" above)
  (`1x-catalog-dependency-cycles` preflight queries `tv.template_resource_id`).

### ADR 0032 and merging (21 October; branch `refactor/catalog-releases`, not pushed)

Decided in ADR 0032: no catalog types, editing as a switch per installation, receiving a release is
a merge into the next version, releases move through the Exchange, a container registry or a file.

| Change                                                                                                                                                          | Where                                                                                         | Coverage                                                                                      |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ADR 0032 rewritten around the user's jobs, with the checks and both spikes as evidence                                                                          | `docs/adr/0032-catalog-sources-releases-and-merging.md`                                       | ➖ docs                                                                                       |

Full runs: core integration 1161 green; app/MCP/REST/guards green except `DataPreservationMigrationIT`.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- `checkContractVersionAlignment` fails on `release/2.0` itself (checked on `a406d5410`): it cannot read
  the editor's `file:` dependency on the local contract archive. It passes again once the suite
  depends on a published contract.
- `DataPreservationMigrationIT` fails on `release/2.0` itself (checked on `a406d5410`): the 1.x
  preflight `1x-catalog-dependency-cycles` (from #1116) queries `tv.template_resource_id`, which the
  RC1 schema does not have.
- Removing a resource row leaves references to it from the same version pointing at an identity that
  version no longer has (`document_templates.theme_resource_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` on
  `catalog_resources`, not on the row), and the resource graph drops such a reference instead of
  reporting it as missing (it joins the target in the current version). `CatalogUpgradeAnalyzer`
  only checks other catalogs. The merge checks it itself.
- `DeleteCatalogRelease`'s doc says the deployment gate does not exist yet; it does (`ReleaseInUse`).
- (Done 21 October.) The import page checks `TEMPLATE_EDIT`, the import command `CATALOG_MANAGE`.
- `RegisterCatalog` upserts over a catalog with the same key without checking its type (unverified
  that it overwrites an authored one).
- The Exchange refuses any number below the highest published (`PublicationPolicy`), though a suite
  comment says an older one may be sent.
- (Done 21 October.) `catalog_releases.released_by` is never written.

**Noticed in slice 2, left alone:**

- Moving resources between catalogs (`ListRelocatableResources`, `CatalogResourceMovePlanner`, the
  organise page's `type === 'authored'`) and `HouseTheme` still decide by type in core.
- `ListCatalogsForManagement.pendingChanges` is computed for AUTHORED catalogs only, so a received
  catalog changed here shows no "pending changes" hint in the catalog list.
- An outdated-schema ZIP imported from the UI is decided as AUTHORED (migrate after confirming) even
  when it creates a received catalog; the fingerprint of a migrated release was not checked.
- `ResourceGraphHandler` and `CatalogOrganiseHandler` still send `catalogs[].type` to their pages.
- Many docs (`catalog-exchange-installation.md`, `catalog-release-model-v2.md`, …) still speak of
  subscribed and authored catalogs; `docs/mcp.md`, `catalog-versioning.md` and
  `catalog-workspace-navigation.md` were updated.

**Noticed in slice 3, left alone:**

- (Done 21 October, snapshot schema 3.) A restored catalog that had a source comes back without its source and, now, without a release
  (a restore keeps no releases; before, a subscribed one recorded its installed version again).
- `UsedCatalogDeclarer` now declares a received catalog that can be changed by its next version, so a
  dependent's release names that draft (pinning its base when it has nothing of its own).
- `ExchangeCatalogInstaller.requireNoLocalConflict` still refuses an Exchange install into a catalog
  made here under the same key, even in the same namespace; ADR 0032 would let it receive.
- REST `importCatalog` answers an aborted import with `IllegalStateException` (as `installCatalogRelease`
  does), not a problem type of its own.
- `ImportCatalogZip` parses an archive twice on the fast-forward path (as before).
- `EnsureSubscribedCatalog` keeps its name; it serves the catalogs Epistola supplies.
- `docs/exchange/README.md`'s stencil-version-conflict and RENUMBER sections still speak of AUTHORED
  MERGE / SUBSCRIBED / REPLACE; stencil versions are gone in 2.0, so the section is stale as a whole.

`checkMigrationVersions` green.

**Noticed in the merge foundation, left alone:**

- The contract needs a `token` on `CatalogDraftMergePreview` and `MergeIntoCatalogDraftRequest`, and
  probably its own problem type for an outdated preview (REST maps to `conflict` for now). The REST
  preview now lists settings entries with `resourceType` `catalog`; check the contract allows it.
- `NextVersionChanges` compares resources only: a next version whose only change is a catalog setting
  (default theme, presets, declarations) is moved to a newer received release, losing it (by reading).
- An entry both sides changed the same way is SAME only when both fingerprint alike; a draft holding a
  received release's content in this suite's form and the release in its publisher's form compare
  unequal, so such an entry is a CONFLICT (the person picks either; nothing is lost).
- A font with bundled (classpath) faces has no bytes in a release's retained content, so a merge
  taking it would be refused; only the system catalog has such fonts, and it cannot be changed.
- (Done 21 October.) `ktlintCheck` fails on `ListDocuments.kt` (dangling KDoc) and `BindingTypes.kt` (mixed `&&`/`||`),
  neither touched here.

| Docs: `catalog-versioning.md` (receiving keeps, lineage, missing base, complete fingerprint, settings), `mcp.md`, changelog fragments                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `docs/`, `changelog/unreleased/`                                                                                                                     | ➖ docs                                                                                                                                                                                    |

**Decisions where the spec left room:** a received id held by another release _or an open draft_ of the catalog refuses (a draft's id becomes its release's id later); a base found by lineage that is not installed and whose fingerprint nobody named counts as no common release (nothing to name to get), not as missing; the missing base's fingerprint is the one the merged release's line names, checked against the installed release by equality, else by its content; the "behind" change list keeps the release-below fallback as a display only; local releases record their base's fingerprint too; `release_entries.fingerprint` keeps storing V3 (REST release detail reads it); the rendering-defaults refusal is a suite-only problem type, like `catalog-schema-too-new`.

**Noticed in phase B, left alone:**

- The contract's `CatalogDraftMergeResource.resourceType` is a free string ("as the portable catalog format names it"), so `catalog` for settings entries is accepted by the schema but is not a portable resource type, and contract ADR 0005 still says the draft's settings are not merged in the alpha. The contract should name the `catalog` entries (or the suite drop them from REST).
- Template descriptions (wire v8) are not stored by the suite: no column, `CatalogContentBuilder` emits none, the installer drops them. A received release keeps it in its retained content, but a draft written from it loses it, and a re-export of a draft does too. Same for a variant's `metadata` (not checked). The complete fingerprint makes such a draft differ from the release where V3 said equal, so a SAME may show as a CONFLICT.
- `PreviewCatalogUpgrade`, `RegisterCatalog`, `UpgradeCatalog` and `installed_resource_fingerprints` still use the V3 per-resource form from the source (both sides alike, so consistent, but blind to v8 additions).
- The behind list's per-release changes compare retained contents, a received release in its publisher's form against a local one in this suite's, so they may list a resource as modified that nobody changed (display only).
- `checkContractVersionAlignment` still cannot read the editor's `file:` contract dependency.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- The image component has no `namespace`/`registry` props, so an image reference into another namespace cannot say which catalog it means; the contract should add them (or declare images tenant-global).
- Import resolves a qualified reference by catalog key alone; an explicit namespace that differs from the tenant's catalog of that key is not checked at the reference (only the catalog dependencies are, by full name).
- A producer that writes a redundant qualifier (the owner's own namespace or registry) on a template's theme, a code-list binding or a default theme gets it dropped on a round trip (they are derived), so that resource fingerprints differently; JSON references keep theirs.
- Resources and default themes with references into another namespace build differently from the content releases retained before this change, so on installations of this branch such a next version shows them as changed once.
- A theme's own-catalog font references keep their `catalogKey` on export (templates and stencils drop it); unchanged.
- The merge's handling of the default theme (`MergeInputs.DefaultTheme`) reads it by catalog key and ignores the qualifier; not touched here (a separate follow-up).

No TypeScript changed.

**Decisions where the spec left room:** the Exchange is listed as a release registry when the deployment enables Exchange, the tenant has an active connection and either the installing or the publishing feature is on; container-kind and Exchange-kind refusals are translated from their commands' validation codes in each kind (the existing commands and UI messages are unchanged); a version the registry lacks is 404 `not-found`, a wrong archive 400 `bad-request`, a draft, a key held here, or publishing/installing switched off 409 `conflict`; `release-number-taken` for imports applies wherever an archive is imported (also `installCatalogRelease`, `installRegistryRelease`), though the contract scopes the slug to publishing; `released_by_installation` is this installation when the metadata names it, so a release imported between two tenants of one installation reads "Released here"; restored snapshots keep the source's auth type but not its credential (snapshots carry no secrets); snapshot schema bumped to 3 so an older suite refuses rather than drops releases; `CatalogVersionReleasedException` became `CatalogReleaseIsReleasedException`; the draft page template is `draft.html`.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- The contract describes `release-number-taken` for publishing only, and `importCatalog`/`installRegistryRelease` say `conflict` for "one number names one release"; the suite now answers `release-number-taken` there (as asked). The contract should name it on those operations.
- A container registry records no release id or base, so `listRegistryReleases` answers them null; the publish could write them as annotations.
- The fake Exchange lists no fingerprints, so the Exchange kind's listing is untested for them.
- `UpdateConsumerRequest.clearExpiresAt` and empty `description`/`contact`: the suite does not implement `ConsumersApi`.
- `catalog-rendering-defaults-too-new` on `installCatalogRelease` is covered by the shared handler, not by its own IT (a source serving a too-new release is not fixtured).
- A restored source that needs a credential has none until it is given again; there is no UI hint for that state.
- A restored catalog's releases are recorded through the receive path, so `notes` and `released_at` are put back after; deployments and publications of releases are not in a snapshot.
- `CatalogVersionId` in KDoc names the contract's old schema name.

Full runs after part 3 (`--no-watch-fs`): core integration 1235 (1 skipped), core unit 1016, app integration 761 (759 green, `DataPreservationMigrationIT` ×2 known), app unit 281, MCP integration 89, rest-api 34, guards 28, demo integration 35 and unit 45, `checkMigrationVersions` and `ktlintCheck` green. "Failed before" runs used a worktree at `c6be80e4a` with the new tests (new-API assertions dropped where they did not compile there).

**Decisions where the spec left room:** merge parents are written as `{version, fingerprint}` objects (a bare version string is read too), so a merge parent names its release by fingerprint like `release.base`; a release on a line that records no lineage (no base, no merges, or not installed) is taken to contain every release numbered below it, for `behind` only (never for a merge base), so releases received from archives without `release.base` do not all turn behind; the dependency entry type is `catalogDependency` keyed by the catalog key (the contract's `resourceKey` is at most 50 characters, so `dependency/<key>` under `catalog` could overflow and the full name never fits); declarations nothing refers to are **kept** after a merge, following editing (`UsedCatalogDeclarer`/`MissingDependencyDeclarer` only ever add), not dropped; only references the merge broke are refused (one that did not resolve in the draft before stays for releasing to refuse); the reference check also runs before writing, because a resource whose own reference is missing fails in the installer with an unhelpful JDBC message; `needs` lists entries referenced by either side's value among the entries the merge changes or asks about.

**Contract follow-ups:** `CatalogDraftMergeResource` has no `needs` (REST does not return it); `resourceType` documents `catalog` settings incl. `dependencies`, and should name `catalogDependency` keyed by catalog key; `merge-refused` describes only a removal still in use, not an unresolved reference; ADR 0005's open question (a release recording what it merged) is answered in the suite's metadata namespace only, a portable `release.merged` would let other producers say it.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- A default theme of another catalog is stored by identity; a theme that only a pinned release still holds (removed from every version) has no identity left, so it cannot be set (`SetCatalogDefaultTheme` refuses it as not in the release).
- `release.metadata` is limited to 8 KiB serialized; a release merging very many releases (over ~70) would exceed it.
- A release removing what the draft uses when it is not a conflict (outcome `THEIRS`) is refused with no choice to offer; the only way on is to stop using it first. Same for a reference the draft broke itself.
- `CatalogMergeHandlerHtmxTest`'s goes-with case was not run against the old code (it asserts new markup).

Full runs (`--no-watch-fs`): core integration 1246 (1 skipped), core unit 1016, app integration 769 (767 green, `DataPreservationMigrationIT` ×2 known), app unit 281, MCP integration 91, rest-api 34, guards 28, demo integration 35 and unit 45, `checkMigrationVersions` and `ktlintCheck` green. No migration; bundled catalogs unchanged; no TypeScript changed.

**Decisions where the spec left room:** merged content is bound by `MergedContentReader` before planning and carried as `MergeResolution.Content(value)`, so the merger only turns it into a write (`WholeResourceMerger` stays an object; a per-type merger can still combine); the portable validator runs with the draft's and the release's resources as context and its not-found findings (`CATALOG_RESOURCE_REFERENCE_MISSING`, `TEMPLATE_THEME_NOT_FOUND`, `STENCIL_REFERENCE_NOT_FOUND`) dropped, so a reference the result lacks is `merge-refused` with what to choose, not a validation error; the field path is `mergedContent.<type/key>/<path>` with `/` between members (`…/variants[2]/key`); merged content's bytes (an image's, a font's faces) are whatever the sides have; a merged dependency needs both `releaseVersion` and `releaseFingerprint`, or `releaseId` naming a draft of that catalog, never both; for later merges a merged entry counts as the draft's change (held as the release has it, like a kept one); the merge records it as `MERGED` in `catalog_draft_merges.resolutions`; REST drops top-level null members of a side (the generated type holds no nulls; the contract says null and absent mean the same); the UI's Compare is read-only and the dialog keeps ours/theirs.

**Contract follow-up (done):** the contract's `entry` pattern for `getCatalogDraftMergeSides` is now `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9-]{0,63}/[^/]+$` (the generator had split the old `*/`, so no entry matched); `CatalogMergedContentApiIT` reads the sides over HTTP (200 with the three sides, 404 `not-found`, 409 `merge-base-missing`, 400 `validation-error`) and the direct-controller workaround is gone.

**Noticed, left alone:**

- ~~The draft's page shows readiness as one hint, not per row~~ — merged content is now badged per row (follow-up below); other not-ready changes still share the one hint.
- `installCatalogRelease` and `installRegistryRelease` answer `release-number-taken` through the shared `CatalogReleaseTakenException` mapping (round 1), verified by reading; only `importCatalog` and the registry publish have their own IT.
- Merged presets are validated when written (`SetCatalogPresets`), after the reference check, so content that is both invalid and unresolved is refused as unresolved.

- REST `getCatalogChanges` (`CatalogChangesDto`, contract-generated) has no field for merged content awaiting review; it needs a contract change.

Build state: generation, epistola-core (main + tests), quality, testing, web compile; MCP
(stencil tools) and rest-api do not, so `apps/*` cannot run. Core: 1956 tests, 79 failing before the
fixes recorded above (v7 remote fixtures, undeclared `catalogDependencies`, `v5:` fixture
fingerprints, `ResourceDetailDeserializationTest` v7, bundled system catalog fingerprint drift) —
all belong to steps 2–3 below. Generation: 679 green.

Still open in this step: UI (stencil page, usage page, catalog page) and editor TS renames (`stencilId`→`stencilKey`,
`assetId`→`imageKey`) — both wait for the REST step, since `apps/*` compile only with `rest-api`;
stencil docs rewritten.

Changelog: fragments `stencils-by-reference`, `declared-catalog-dependencies` and `catalog-wire-v8`
(identity, key renames, data example keys, `v5:` fingerprints, bundled releases) written.

Verified 17 October: `./gradlew test` green across all modules (apps/epistola 859, core 1977, generation 679, MCP 70, quality 113, guards 19, demo 78, rest-api 29, loadtest 7, support modules, web 78).

Done in the editor step below: the editor TS (`stencilId`→`stencilKey`, `assetId`→`imageKey`, the save/preview/contract/stencil routes below, lexical recursion), and `StencilPlaceholderPlaywrightUiTest` waits for it. A temporary shim in `editor-boot.js` answers the editor's stencil callbacks from the working copy. Routes changed: variant save `…/variants/{v}/draft` → `…/variants/{v}/content`; preview takes `releaseVersion` instead of `versionId`; `PATCH …/contract/draft` → `PATCH …/contract`; stencil `/versions*` and `/upgrade` removed, `GET/PUT …/content` and `POST …/ready` added.

### Upgrade job (17 October)

Decided with the user 17 October: **one release per catalog from the latest published versions,
deployed nowhere** (operators deploy).

Next (b): every SQL site names its version (`versionAt`), guarded by a source scan; then (c) drop the
transitional trigger, the old keys and the one-per-catalog rule, retarget references to identities,
and the draft lifecycle (create, delete, `behind`, release with several drafts).

### Catalog page redone: next version, versions with their own pages (17 October)

User feedback: still confusing; 99% of the time people work on the next release, based on the latest published version, numbered only when published; make the advanced cases explicit; navigate from the catalog page to each released and draft version; catalog details belong to a version. Use cases to support well: (1) new catalog → first template → used from an application such as GZAC; (2) keep improving the next version; (3) an intermediate release while the next version is unfinished.

Noticed, not touched: catalog details (name, description, keywords, presentation, license) are stored once per catalog and shared by every open draft; a release freezes its own copy in its manifest — they should belong to each draft in core (presentation names images, which are per version); `EmbeddingBridgeUiTest` "clicking a boosted row link" failed once in the full UI run and passed on rerun (flaky, unrelated); bringing an in-between version's change into the next version is still by hand; the editor's pickers offer every catalog's themes, stencils, images, code lists and fonts (current versions), while a release renders them from the declared catalog's locked release — so a resource only in that catalog's draft passes the editor and fails or differs at render; the page now builds the catalog twice per render (`GetCatalogResourceChanges` and `GetCatalogBorrowing` each build it); a dependency made live along with a catalog becomes the highest version that dependency serves, so the dependency's own templates render with it — with one live version per catalog the Live table shows the version still needed by another catalog as a second served version; the release-render class doc (`ReleaseRenderSource.kt:80-84`, "a release does not lock the catalogs it borrows from") contradicts its code; the subscribed catalog page keeps its flat contents table (it carries the install status); taking a published fix over into the next version is still by hand: core copies a release into a draft only whole (`CreateCatalogVersion` exports the release and imports it), so a per-resource take-over needs a selective import; "stay on Shared v2.3" as a lasting setting is not built (publish-along can be unticked per publish, and the preview still shows Shared's work while it is unticked); the variant ready controls stay on the template page (marking checks the variant early) — whether to drop ready marks from the UI is the user's call; publishing and making live are two commands (release, then one deploy per ticked environment), not one transaction — a refused deploy leaves the version published and says so. Workspace shell: there is no "installed only because another catalog uses it" state (an unmet dependency is refused at install), so the switcher shows every authored and subscribed catalog except the system catalog; the part lists keep their own catalog filter select inside a catalog, which reads oddly there; the tenant-wide resource lists (all templates, all themes) are reachable only by URL now; the editor's bar has no template switcher yet; `DriftRatchetTest`'s "handlers/*.kt declaring another package" counter also counts sub-packages whose directory matches (so the workspace files went into the flat package). The lists' "from catalogs it depends on" use declared dependencies only: a catalog used but not declared yet (declared at publish) is not shown there; a draft's Based on can name a published version that was since deleted. The deploy refusal's "deploy with what it needs" offer (`DeployRefusalView.deployableAlong`) can no longer appear, since nothing is refused for being undeployed; left in place. Releases deployed along before this change stay deployed explicitly. Kotlin methods taking value-class parameters cannot be called from templates (name-mangled): precompute strings in the model. Deleting a theme in a catalog's working copy clears the theme reference of templates in other catalogs that use it (the database link), so the dependent no longer lists it as used and nothing warns; a removed used resource is therefore not caught by the per-use check. The per-use check is per resource (a stencil's interface), not per instance (which placeholders a given instance fills). Stage 1 leaves untestable through commands: a next version that is behind (only promotion, stage 2, or migrated data makes one), so `acknowledgedBehind` on the next version has no command-level test, and `ReleaseBaseTest`'s "measured against the release's own base" case returns with promotion. `CatalogDraftsMigrationTest` and `SeveralDraftsMigrationTest` now migrate to their own migration only. The contract text says a too-low release number is refused with 409; the REST layer maps `CatalogReleaseVersionException` to 400 (`CATALOG_RELEASE_VERSION_INVALID`) — align the contract text to 400. A failed export download (including a refused fat export) answers JSON to a plain browser GET, as every export error did before: the person sees raw JSON; the export dialog should check first or render an error page. A successful bundle import lands on the catalog without saying which dependencies came along (the Dependencies page shows them).

### 1.x → 2.0 upgrade test (19–20 October)

Real 1.5.0 image (amd64, emulated) on its own database, seeded over its REST API and UI: the demo
catalog imported as authored, activations per environment with production behind test, versions
2 and 3 with one archived, unpublished drafts, a never-published template, a 1.x catalog release
(5.21.0), a cross-catalog template on another catalog's theme and stencil, an unpublished contract
change, a stencil published past the version templates pin, a second tenant with nothing
published, completed documents, and 300–400 requests still queued. Dumped, then upgraded with this
branch's jar, several times from the same dump.

Held up: all 16 migrations apply; a `kill -9` mid-migration recovers on restart; the runner releases
the patch after the 1.x release (5.21.1) and 1.0.0, records subscribed releases, pins the
cross-catalog declaration; releases hold what was last published, drafts the unpublished work
(contract changes included), the never-published template only the draft; queued requests fail
with "resubmit it"; 1.x documents still download; the 1.x API key works; generation is refused
until a deploy and works after, cross-catalog too; tenant home names environments without a
catalog; editors open upgraded content without errors; `upgrade-1x-history` and
`upgrade-2.0-template-content` are written.

Fixed (`d7cf75ccf`, `e863a77de`, each with a failing-before test): queued requests logged an error
with a stack trace each (1,601 lines for 400); a catalog using a catalog the tenant lacks waited
with "dependency names no namespace" instead of naming it. Docs: which documents change, and
`deployCatalogVersion`.

Open, for discussion:

- **Live output changes at the upgrade.** An environment on an older 1.x version gets what was last
  published once deployed (production on v1 serves test's v2), and a stencil pinned to an older
  version renders as it is now. Verified in a generated PDF. The older content is not kept, so it
  cannot be deployed instead. Option: cut a release per distinct environment activation set.
- **No guard against a live 1.x node.** 2.0 migrated while a 1.x node heartbeated; the 1.x node then
  fails every call (500, nothing written). Option: refuse a breaking migration while `cluster_nodes`
  shows another node seen in the last minute, with an override.
- A refusal for an environment with nothing deployed is 404 `no-release-deployed`.

### Promotion folded into publishing (19 October)

`MakeNextVersion`, its UI dialog and route, and MCP `make_next_version` are gone. Publishing parallel
work asks _After publishing_: everyday work carries on in the next version, or continues from the
release (`ReleaseCatalogVersion.continueEverydayWork`, `NextVersionHandOver`), keeping the old next
version by name or discarding it. Nothing is merged. Covered by `PromotionTest` (both new cases),
`DraftDeploymentTest`, `CatalogDraftsHandlerHtmxTest`, `CatalogVersionMcpToolsIntegrationTest`.
