## Design: catalogs by opaque id, named by registry + namespace + key — decided 16 October

User: a key is not unique per tenant (two `letters` in different namespaces must coexist), and
correctness beats effort. Resources already have opaque ids (`catalog_resources.resource_id`) and
reference each other by them; catalogs get the same treatment.

Schema (verified against the migrated schema, not the migration files):

- `catalogs`: new opaque `catalog_id UUID` (UUIDv7), primary key `(tenant_key, catalog_id)`; column
  `id` renamed `catalog_key`; identity `UNIQUE (tenant_key, registry, namespace, catalog_key)`.
- **Live tables reference the catalog by `catalog_id`** (FK to `catalogs`, `ON DELETE CASCADE` as
  today), replacing `catalog_key`: `catalog_resources`, `themes`, `document_templates`,
  `variant_attribute_definitions`, `assets`, `stencils`, `code_lists`, `fonts` (address
  `UNIQUE (tenant_key, catalog_id, key)`); `catalog_releases` (PK `(tenant_key, catalog_id,
  version)`) and its dependants `release_entries`, `catalog_release_publications`,
  `environment_catalog_deployments`; `catalog_upstream_checks`.
- **History keeps the name it had:** `documents` and `document_generation_requests` (partitioned)
  and `environment_deployment_log` keep `catalog_key` and gain `registry` and `namespace`,
  backfilled; no FK — history names a catalog, it does not point at one.
- Unchanged: `catalog_exchange_bindings` (nobody uses Exchange yet); quality and load-test tables
  (already on `template_resource_id`); old-world tables (no `catalog_key` left).
- DB functions follow: `sync_catalog_resource_identity` (registry rows by `catalog_id`),
  `touch_catalog_content_on_delete` / `_on_variant_delete` (join by `catalog_id`),
  `fill_generation_template_identity` (check).

Code:

- `CatalogId(tenantId, registry, namespace, key)`; resource ids (`TemplateId`, `StencilId`,
  `ThemeId`, …) carry it. A bare `CatalogKey` is no longer an identity.
- SQL resolves a catalog from its name with one helper (`catalogAt(...)`, like the existing
  `stencilAtAddress(...)`); `catalog_id` never leaves persistence.
- Every catalog exception carries `CatalogName`; a not-found names what was asked for.

Verification: a migration test seeding pre-2.0-shaped rows (two catalogs, resources, releases,
deployments, documents) and checking every row maps to the right `catalog_id`; then the existing
suites; plus a test with two `letters` in different namespaces in one tenant through import,
release, deploy and render.

## Design: stencils by reference (step 2) — decided 16 October

Recorded as **contract ADR 0006** (`epistola-contract` `docs/adr/0006-stencil-instances-by-reference.md`,
commit `9ff4d72` on `feature/v2`, local), status Proposed: parts 1–4 decided, part 5 (isolation) proposed.

Today (mapped 16 October): a stencil instance is an **embedded copy** in the template's stencil node
(`children` slot), with props `stencilId`, `catalogKey`, `version`, `draftVersion`,
`parameterSchemaSnapshot`, `parameterBindings`; placeholder fills live in the copy's `fill` slots.
The renderer only reads the copy (`StencilNodeRenderer`), never the stencil. Upgrading an instance
is `UpdateStencilInTemplate` + `StencilContentReplacer`, which reports `droppedFills`,
`droppedBindings`, `unboundRequired` but blocks on none.

**Decided (16 October, user): no copy.** A template stores an instance as a reference (stencil key,
catalog when not its own), its bindings, and one placeholder node per placeholder it fills. The
stencil is resolved when the template is opened, previewed or rendered — in the same catalog
version, or in the pinned release of a dependency. A stencil change reaches every template at once;
releasing checks every instance against its stencil (unknown fill or unbound required parameter =
error, unknown binding = warning), like a compile step. No digests, no held back, no propagation.
Stencils in stencils work the same way (inner references resolve in the stencil's catalog; the outer
stencil fills the inner one's placeholders and can pass its own placeholders through); the suite
still refuses nested stencils (`rejectUnsupportedNestedStencilAuthoring`); **decided 16 October: stencils in stencils must be possible** — remove the refusal and verify the editor in the wiring step.

**Fill scope: lexical (decided 16 October, user).** A fill sees what was in scope where it was
written; a stencil's interface is exactly its placeholders and parameters.

Upgrade from 1.x must not change what renders (user requirement). Plan, not built (ADR 0006 table):
no expression rewriting. Where 1.x fills read the instance's alias, the stencil's placeholder gets
`passParameters` (and a non-default alias goes onto the fill as `parametersAs`); where 1.x stencil
content reads template data, the stencil gets `readsTemplateData`. Detection parses expressions
(JSONata AST). A 1.x readiness check counts both.

**Placeholder passes parameters (decided 16 October, user):** opt-in per placeholder by the stencil
author; the name is chosen by the using side, defaulting to the author's, else `params`.

**Decided 16 October (user):**

- 1.x instances on an older stencil version are **upgraded automatically** to the latest stencil
  (they render the latest after the upgrade). What no longer fits (dropped fills, unbound required
  parameters — the `StencilInstances` findings) is **reported**: upgrade report and quality findings.
- Copies that are not a normal locked copy (no `stencilId`, or content differing from the pinned
  version beyond fills) become plain content and are reported — collapsing them would lose content.
- A release preview of templates affected by a stencil change comes **later**; prepare for it by
  keeping "templates affected by a stencil change" a query of its own.

**Superseded proposal** (copies + held back; kept for the record):

- A stencil has one working copy (content + parameter schema) and a ready mark; `stencil_versions` goes.
- An instance keeps its embedded copy and loses `version`/`draftVersion`.
- **Marking a stencil ready moves every instance in the same catalog** whose template it does not
  break (`StencilContentReplacer`; breaking = it would drop a fill or binding, or leave a newly
  required parameter unbound). Breaking instances are **held back** on their old copy, listed in
  stencil usage, and block the release until the variant is edited.
- Cross-catalog instances move when the dependency pin moves (to confirm).
- Upgrade from 1.x: the first release is cut from today's copies (renders exactly as before); then
  instances behind the latest stencil are moved in the draft, breaking ones held back.

Goes with it (step 2 scope): `stencil_versions` and its commands (`CreateStencilVersion`,
`UpdateStencilDraft`, `PublishStencilVersion`, `ArchiveStencilVersion`), `PublishVersion`'s
published-stencil check, the interim `stencilVersionPinFindings`, `MultipleStencilVersionsInUseException`

- `FindStencilVersionExportConflicts`, `OnStencilConflict` and the renumber plumbing, the usage
  page's `UpgradeBlockReason`, the client-side bulk upgrade (`stencil-detail.js`), MCP
  `list_stencil_versions`/`get_stencil_version`/`update_stencil_draft`, editor `draft-hydration.ts`
  and `upgrade-refs.ts`, REST's draft/publish-based stencil working copy.

Decided (16 October, user):

- An instance stores a **content digest** and an **interface digest** (placeholders + parameter
  schema). Different content digest = behind. Same interface digest = safe to move. Different
  interface digest = check the instance (does it use a removed or retyped fill or binding, or leave
  a newly required parameter unbound) — only then held back.
- Cross-catalog instances move when the dependency pin moves, "like upgrading a Java library":
  preview the breaking instances before moving the pin; breaking ones are held back and block the
  release.
- While template versions still exist, a stencil update writes into template drafts.

Proposed, awaiting the user — what the template author sees on stencil ready:
A silent update · B update and reset templates to modified · C mark stale, update on request ·
**D (recommended)**: compatible copies move, template keeps its ready mark with a non-blocking
"updated by stencil X" note in history and release changes; breaking ones held back; the stencil
author sees a preview ("updates 37 templates, 2 held back") when marking ready. C fits only if
template and stencil owners must not change each other's work.

Open questions: see "Open decisions".

## Design: catalog versions (step 4, ADR 0002) — proposed 17 October, not built

The contract addresses every resource as `…/catalogs/{registry}/{namespace}/{key}/versions/{v}/…`:
reads on any version, writes and ready marks on drafts only (`409` on a released one). Several
drafts may be open; a draft is an independent copy with a fixed base, can be `behind`, is renumbered
with acknowledged warnings, and releasing it is a state change of the same version. Dependencies
are per version (`…/versions/{v}/dependencies`).

**Identity across versions, content per draft.** `catalog_resources.resource_id` stays the one
identity of a resource in every version (the quality ledger, generation history, relocation and
release entries already key on it). What a draft holds is stored per draft:

- New `catalog_versions (tenant_key, version_id UUID, catalog_id, version, status draft|released,
  base_version, source_version, released_at…)`; `UNIQUE (catalog_id, version)`. A released version
  keeps its row; its content is the retained release content (`release_entries`), so its live rows
  are dropped on release and reads of it go through `ReleaseContentAssembler`.
- Every live content table keys on `(tenant_key, version_id, resource_id)` instead of
  `(tenant_key, resource_id)`, and its address `UNIQUE (version_id, key)`. References between
  content rows (a template's theme, variants of a template) carry `version_id` in their FKs, so a
  reference always resolves in the same draft.
- `catalogAt(t, c)` (175 SQL sites) splits into `catalogAt` (catalog-level tables: releases,
  deployments, upstream checks) and `draftAt(t, c, v)` (content). `CatalogId` gains an optional
  version; without one, a write resolves to the catalog's only open draft and is refused when there
  are several (so callers that do not know about versions keep working while one draft is open).
- Creating a draft copies the content rows of its source (a draft's live rows, or a released
  version's retained content) under the new `version_id`, keeping every `resource_id`.
- Subscribed catalogs have no drafts: their installed release is their content (one implicit
  version row, read-only).
- `catalog_dependencies` moves to the version (declarations are per draft, as the contract says).

**First, remove template and contract versions (4a).** With catalog versions, `template_versions`
(draft/published per variant) and `contract_versions` are a second, conflicting version axis. A
variant holds one model per draft, a template one data contract per draft; publishing becomes the
per-resource ready mark (`markTemplateReady`, `markVariantReady`, `markStencilReady`), a stored
digest cleared by an edit. Upgrade rule (plan): the last published template version becomes the
first release's content; an open draft becomes the open draft's content; older versions are lost.

**4a in detail (decided 17 October; contract `markVariantReady`/`markTemplateReady`/
`markStencilReady`/`markCatalogReady`/`getCatalogChanges` are the authority — the ready mark stays,
`docs/v2-simplify-first` is superseded on this point):**

- Storage: `template_variants.template_model` (+ `referenced_paths`), `document_templates.data_model`
  and `data_examples`. Backfill: the open draft, else the latest published version (same for the
  contract). `template_versions`/`contract_versions` stay as unread history (data is never dropped);
  the pre-2.0 generation path for already-queued requests keeps reading them.
- Ready: `resource_ready_marks (tenant_key, resource_id, part, digest)`; `part` is the variant key for
  a template (a variant's readiness includes the template's shared fields and data contract, as the
  contract says), `''` otherwise. The digest is computed **in SQL** by one view,
  `resource_content_digests`, over the stored content of every resource type, so a mark is a copy of
  the view's value, an edit invalidates it without bookkeeping, and the upgrade backfills it in SQL.
- Changed vs base stays `GetCatalogResourceChanges` (canonical per-resource fingerprints against the
  base release); a release is refused while a changed resource is not ready.
- Upgrade semantics: a variant with no open draft is marked ready (its published content was
  releasable before); a variant with an open draft is not (its draft was not). Same for contracts.
- Names: `UpdateVariantContent`, `MarkVariantReady`, `MarkTemplateReady`, `MarkStencilReady`,
  `MarkCatalogReady`, `UpdateDataContract`; the version commands and queries go.

**Alternatives rejected:** a draft as its own `catalogs` row (releases and deployments are per
catalog, so they would need a second id anyway); one live working copy with other drafts as stored
snapshots checked out on edit (writes to two drafts at once would swap content back and forth);
one open draft at a time (correct but not ADR 0002).

**Step 3 status (17 October):** core 2008 tests, 0 failed; MCP 71 and quality 113 green. Unsigned
commits to sign once 1Password works again: `d863b7c93`, `f760880aa` (and any later ones marked
`N` in `git log --format='%h %G? %s'`).

## Design: several drafts (step 4b) — decided 17 October, deferred (see Plan)

Refines the step 4 design above against the schema as it is after 4a (dumped from a migrated
scratch database, 17 October).

1. **`catalog_versions` replaces `catalog_drafts`** (that migration is not in `main`, so it is
   rewritten): `(tenant_key, version_id UUID, catalog_id, version, kind draft|installed,
   base_version, created_from, created_at)`, PK `(tenant_key, version_id)`, `UNIQUE (tenant_key,
   catalog_id, version)`. An authored catalog has one row per open draft; a subscribed catalog one
   `installed` row, read-only, numbered by its installed release. A released version has no row:
   its content is the retained release content, read through `ReleaseContentAssembler`. A number is
   unique across drafts and releases (checked by the commands, as renumber already does).
2. **Content rows belong to a version.** `themes`, `document_templates`, `template_variants`,
   `variant_attribute_definitions`, `code_lists`, `code_list_entries`, `assets`, `fonts`,
   `font_variants`, `stencils` drop `catalog_id` and gain `version_id`: PK `(tenant_key, version_id,
   resource_id)` (children: plus their own key), address `UNIQUE (tenant_key, version_id, key)`.
   `catalog_id` is dropped rather than kept so every SQL site not yet converted fails loudly instead
   of returning one row per draft.
3. **Identity stays per catalog.** `catalog_resources (tenant_key, resource_id, catalog_id, type,
   key)` is unchanged: one resource has one identity in every version and every release (quality,
   generation history, relocation and release entries key on it).
4. **References name identities, never rows.** FKs that pointed at a content row's
   `(tenant_key, resource_id)` point at `catalog_resources` instead: a template's theme, an
   attribute's code list, a font face's asset, the tenant's default theme, and the history and
   feature tables (`quality_findings`, `quality_finding_ignores`, `load_test_runs`,
   `template_versions`, `contract_versions`, `stencil_versions`, `environment_activations`). A
   reference resolves in the version being read when it is in the same catalog, and in the
   dependency's locked release when it is in another (ADR 0003) — as rendering already does.
5. **Per-version state:** `resource_ready_marks` and `catalog_dependencies` gain `version_id`; the
   `resource_content_digests` view carries it.
6. **SQL:** `catalogAt(t, c)` stays for catalog-level tables (releases, deployments, upstream
   checks, identity). Content uses `versionAt(t, c)`: the catalog's only content version (its one
   draft, or its installed row), and is refused when there are several, so a caller that does not
   name a version keeps working while one draft is open. Callers that name one use
   `versionNamed(t, c, v)`. `CatalogId` stays as it is; a version-aware id is added where the
   contract's `…/versions/{v}` reaches a command.
7. **Lifecycle:** creating a draft copies the content rows of its source (another draft, or a
   released version's retained content) under a new `version_id`, keeping every `resource_id` and
   asset binary. Releasing a draft records the release and, when no draft above it exists, renumbers
   that same version row to the next patch (the content rows stay put, as one draft works today);
   otherwise its rows are deleted. Deleting a draft deletes its rows.
8. **Slices:** (a) schema + backfill (every existing catalog's rows to its one version) + helpers,
   behaviour unchanged with one draft; (b) convert every SQL site, guarded by the full suite;
   (c) create, delete, `behind`, release with several drafts, renumber over several; (d) REST
   `createCatalogVersion`/`deleteCatalogVersion` and version-scoped writes, MCP, UI.

## Plan

0. Contract snapshot where CI can fetch it; rewrite the suite plan against ADRs 0001–0005.
1. Rename pass (in progress).
2. Remove versions from resources (stencils first); keep the ready mark.
3. Catalog versions: draft/released, several drafts, `behind`, renumber, delete; upgrade rule: last
   published → first release, draft → open draft, older versions lost.
4. Identity: registry + namespace + key; configuration; key-rule migration.
5. Wire v8 import/export; bundled catalogs regenerated.
6. Dependencies: declared per version, pinned at release, release-time checks.
7. Deployments: several releases per environment, dependency check, selection by `version` +
   environment.
8. REST to the spec; UI; MCP.
9. Upgrade job on a 1.x-shaped database; cleanup; docs; demo catalog; ADR 0028.

**Order revised 17 October (after 4a):** REST to the contract spec next, then the UI, then 4b
(several drafts). Reason: `rest-api` does not compile (554 errors), so `apps/*` do not, so the
guards that live there (`MediatorWiringTest`, `AuthorizationCoverageTest`, `DomainBoundaryTest`,
`UiRestApiSeparationTest`) have not run since the contract catch-up began. The contract's
`…/versions/{catalogVersion}/…` paths are implemented for one open draft (the working copy) and
released versions (read from release content); `createCatalogVersion` for a second draft is
refused until 4b, which then adds `version_id` without an API change.

**Order revised again 17 October (after the editor step):** 4b is deferred to after the upgrade
job, the demo catalog and deployments. Measured scope: 193 `catalogAt` sites in 110 files, plus
every tenant-wide read (usages, search, resource graph, relocation, quality scans) needs a rule for
which draft it shows — likely "the highest open draft" rather than a refusal. The API already has
the version paths and refuses a second draft with 501, so 4b adds no API change later, while the
upgrade job decides whether an existing installation's data comes across correctly and the demo
catalog blocks the PR. Next: (1) upgrade job — backfill declarations, rewrite stored v7 release
members; (2) demo catalog on 2.0 (declares `system`, shows ready/renumber/dependencies);
(3) deployments with several releases per environment; (4) validate on URL registration; then 4b.

**17 October, afternoon:** done since the reorder: upgrade job (declarations + first release, deploy
nowhere — decided with the user), demo catalog on 2.0, validation on URL installs. Now: step 7,
deployments with several releases per environment, the dependency check and `includeDependencies`,
and generation's `version` as a selector combinable with `environmentKey` (contract); then 4b.

## Open decisions

- ~~May MCP release and discard drafts?~~ **Yes** (user, 17 October): MCP may release a catalog version
  and create, renumber and delete drafts, under the same permissions as the UI. Deploying stays in the
  UI (`docs/mcp.md`).

- Registry and namespace for the bundled system and demo catalogs (e.g. `exchange.epistola.app` / `epistola`)?

- Keep `paramsAlias` now that lexical scope makes it unnecessary for nesting?

- Stencils: inserting a stencil that was never marked ready — allowed, from its working copy?

- ~~Stencil pins on import: stopgap, or remove stencil versions now?~~ Removed (stencil step).
- Catalog identity in the schema: registry/namespace in every key, or a surrogate key?
- Default registry when none is configured (the installation's host?).
- Publish contract snapshots to Sonatype so `release/2.0` CI runs?
- Where `progress.md` lives once the catch-up branch merges (committed, or kept local).

## Notes

- Run Gradle with `--no-watch-fs`; worktrees need `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm build`.
- Don't trust a background command's exit code; check for `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`/`FAILED` and the
  test-result XML timestamps.

### ADR 0007: themes per catalog (20 October, in progress)

Contract `feature/v2` ADR 0007 (accepted): a catalog's own `defaultTheme`; a catalog declares its
`presets`, each theme styles them, a catalog's available presets include its dependencies'; the
tenant default theme stays only as a **house theme**, never read while rendering (a new catalog
starts with it; "apply to all catalogs" sets it).

Plan, in order:

1. Contract: `CatalogInfo.defaultTheme` (theme reference, cross-catalog qualified) and
   `CatalogInfo.presets` (key, name, description) in wire v8 (2.0 is unreleased, optional members),
   Kotlin model + binding + canonical form (absent members leave fingerprints unchanged), TS model,
   validator; API: catalog details read/update carry both. Publish locally (2.0.0-SNAPSHOT).
2. Suite storage: both per catalog version (they go out with each version); migration seeds
   `default_theme` from the tenant default where the catalog's templates inherit, and `presets` from
   the preset keys its themes style; existing releases record the theme they rendered with.
3. Rendering: variant theme → template theme → catalog default → none, in release, draft and
   preview renders; the tenant default is no longer read.
4. Presets: available = own + dependencies'; the editor offers them; a block naming an undeclared
   preset is refused when the catalog declares presets; a theme not styling one is a quality finding.
5. House theme: `CreateCatalog` starts with it; `ApplyHouseTheme` sets every authored catalog's
   default; UI on the tenant/theme page.
6. UI (catalog Details: default theme, presets), MCP, REST, export/import round trip, demo catalog,
   docs, changelog.

ADR 0007 state (20 October): built. Storage + migration, commands/queries, renders (draft,
preview, editor, release), house theme, themes list, Details page, editor presets, preset refusal,
quality finding, MCP, REST (draft update + version DTO), manifest round trip (export, import, install,
parallel work), demo catalog 6.3.0, docs, changelog. Full `test uiTest`: 4635 tests, 1 failure
(`SystemFontsIntegrationTest` set the tenant default theme; now the catalog's) — passes after.
Contract `feature/v2` `173dfef` (published locally as 2.0.0-SNAPSHOT). Left: the REST draft update
cannot clear a default theme through generated clients (null and omitted are the same there).

## Review follow-ups (27 October)

- **Done: a release is a release however it arrived** (user decision, 28 October). `catalog_versions.origin`,
  its two checks, the view `catalog_installed_releases` and the function `catalog_installed_release_id` are
  gone (2.0 chain edited in place); `catalog_current_releases` is the catalog's highest release of any kind,
  and `Catalog` has one release (`latestReleaseVersion`, fingerprint, released at) instead of three
  pointers. Upgrade checks compare against it (`SemVer.isNewer`): a release cut here above the source's
  keeps the catalog current. The Exchange's withdrawn check never reports a release this installation cut.
  Provenance on every release, shown only: `released_via` (released, upload, api, registry, exchange,
  source, bundled, upgrade-1x, snapshot), `released_from`, `released_by` (the installer for a received
  release), `released_at`; on the release page, in `list_catalog_releases` and in tenant snapshots.
  `catalog_upstream_checks` columns renamed by a forward migration (`V20261101001700`). Tests:
  `ReleaseProvenanceTest`, `SemVerTest`, `UpgradeCatalogTest` (mixed catalog, ahead of source: failed
  without `isNewer`), `ExchangeUpstreamCheckTest` (cut here: failed without the authorship check),
  channel assertions in the Exchange, registry, snapshot and 1.x scenario tests, `ReleaseProvenanceUsageTest`
  (guard), `TwoZeroChainEquivalenceIT` (`was`/`new` directives: rows read as the old chain wrote them).
  Not done: REST has no provenance field (the contract has none; no bump).

- **Done: monthly partitions in UTC.** `PartitionMaintenanceScheduler` creates every monthly
  partition (`documents`, `document_content`, `document_generation_requests`, `generation_results`,
  `event_log`, `audit_log`) through `epistola_create_partition`, whose `date` bounds Postgres reads
  against the `timestamptz` key in the session's zone, and pgjdbc takes that from the JVM. A node
  outside UTC created shifted partitions. The create now runs in its own transaction with
  `SET LOCAL TIME ZONE 'UTC'`, and the current month (create and retention cutoff) is computed in
  UTC. `PartitionMaintenanceTimeZoneIT` runs maintenance through Asia/Tokyo connections and failed
  without the fix. Found by the 2.0 chain's equivalence test, which had the same flaw in its
  migration step.
- **Done: the migration step under `bootRun`.** `EPISTOLA_MIGRATION_MODE=migrate` or `--migrate`
  through devtools failed with `migrate,migrate`: the restart calls `main` again with the arguments
  the migration context got, and the launcher appended the mode once more. `runMigration` now drops
  its own options from the arguments first. Proven by
  `MigrationLauncherIntegrationTest` "…already name the mode, as a devtools restart hands them back",
  which failed before. The Helm Job and init container pass the mode by environment variable, with
  no devtools, and were never affected.
- **Done: one install core** (review finding 1). URL, `file:` and `classpath:` sources build the
  release's archive (`ManifestUrlReleaseSource`, migrated fetches) and `ImportCatalogZip` receives it;
  `InstallFromCatalog` and `UpgradeCatalog` are thin adapters. Import semantics for upgrades (user
  decision B): a fast-forward without local changes, recorded beside with them;
  `CatalogHasLocalChangesException` is gone. `DependencyResolver.requireComplete` now runs for every
  received archive. Tests: `SourceInstallChecksTest` (tampered binary, wrong fingerprint, missing pin
  failed on the old core; missing attribute guards the resolver), `UpgradeCatalogTest` on real v1 →
  v2 sources (drop, conflict, beside, unserved binary). A source's archive is a ZIP, imported as any
  other: one install path. Writing it deflates the system catalog's 14 MB of faces, which tripled
  the cost of a tenant's creation; that matters only to tests (a tenant is created rarely in
  production), so the test infrastructure builds a `classpath:` source's archive once per JVM
  (`BundledCatalogArchiveCache` in `modules/testing`, bounded, registered as a test-only
  auto-configuration). `SystemFontBootPerfTest`, ms, base (#1161) → ZIP → ZIP with the test cache:
  first tenant 1669 → 2270–2419 → 2394–2677 (it builds the archive, once per JVM), second tenant 314
  → 893–1051 → 421–441 (what is left is reading and checking the archive, as every import does), boot
  pass 12.7 → 5.1 → 5.3. An in-memory archive handed to the import was tried and taken out: a second
  entry point to the install core. An
  aborted receive now rolls back every resource it wrote (a savepoint), for archives as for sources.
  Gap noted, not fixed: the contract validator checks no variant attribute keys nor a legacy
  `assetId` image prop, which only the old URL core's `DependencyResolver` walk caught.
- **Done: `catalogs.installed_*` as a view** (finding 2, `V20261027100000`). Receiving records the
  release, then `RecordInstalledRelease` moves the next version to it (base, base id, merges
  cleared, a stale baseline forgotten); the trigger `catalog_installed_version` is replaced by one
  that opens the next version on no release. `catalog_installed_releases` walks the next version's
  base chain to the nearest received release, so a release cut here keeps the installed one (as the
  old pointer did). The 1.x installed release and fingerprint reach the runner through
  `catalog_upgrade_queue` (2.0 migration `V20261018201400` edited in place); its fingerprint is never
  adopted, and a release received since (the system catalog at boot) wins. `RegisterCatalog` no
  longer fetches the whole source for a stored baseline, nor writes the release's declarations (on a
  next version based on no release they counted as changes of its own); the preview compares the
  installed release's retained content, the source only when that is not kept. Occurrences in main
  Kotlin: 12 in 9 files (3 writers) → 3 in 2 files (the view's aliases, the queue column). Tests:
  `UpgradeCatalogTest` (second fast-forward, local release keeps installed), `InstalledReleaseRecordTest`
  (queued fingerprint not adopted, received since wins). Dev databases from earlier 2.0 builds need
  a rebuild.
- **Done: the 1.x → 2.0 upgrade scenarios** (finding 10). Each seeds a database as the last 1.x
  release left it and starts the app on it (Flyway with the preflight, then the boot passes), on
  PostgreSQL 17 and 18:

  | Scenario                                                                        | Test                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
  | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | A 1.x database on its last release                                              | `DataPreservationMigrationIT` (RC1 → 1.x → 2.0, PG 17/18), `OneXUpgradeReleasesIT`                                                                                                                                                  |
  | An unfinished 1.x content backfill                                              | `LegacyContentMigrationTest`                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
  | That backfill interrupted after one committed batch, resumed                    | `LegacyContentResumeTest` (PG 17/18): 100 of 150 stamped, no failed migration recorded, the rerun moves the rest once                                                                                                               |
  | Two authored catalogs, one using the other's theme                              | `OneXUpgradeScenariosIT` (PG 17/18 subclasses): a "Published in 1.x" draft only where there is unpublished work, the used catalog declared by name, the release refused naming it until it is released, then pinned to that release |
  | A catalog subscribed to from a URL whose source names its publisher's namespace | `OneXUpgradeScenariosIT`: received at boot into the tenant's catalog, its next version fast-forwarded                                                                                                                               |
  | An environment that activated versions in 1.x                                   | `OneXUpgradeScenariosIT`: nothing deployed, an empty deployment log, the activations in `upgrade-1x-history`, `NoReleaseDeployedException` until the released "Published in 1.x" draft is deployed                                  |

  Shown failing when the behaviour is disabled: the subscribed scenario without the boot pass
  (`ReceiveOneXSubscribedReleases`), the two-catalog scenario without release-time pinning.

- **Fixed, found by the scenarios: a 1.x catalog subscribed to from a URL received its release as a
  second catalog.** 1.x catalogs had no namespace, so the upgrade named a subscribed one in its
  tenant's (`V20261018200100`), while a real source names it in its publisher's. The boot pass
  installed the release under the source's name, a new catalog, and left the tenant's own without a
  release, retried at every boot. It now takes the registry and namespace the source's manifest gives
  it first (it has never had a release, so its namespace can still change), with the drafts that
  declare it; a different key, or a name another catalog already has, fails it, logged and retried.

- 2.0 chain, time zone (2026-10-27): `TwoZeroChainEquivalenceIT` failed in CI (UTC) but passed locally (CET). Two
  causes. (1) Step 5 (`V20261101000500`) bounded the `document_content` partition and grouped documents by month
  in the session time zone pgjdbc takes from the JVM, so a CET migration shifted the bounds by its offset; the
  step now runs with the session in UTC (the old chain had the same flaw). (2) The snapshot rendered times, and
  labels derived from them, in that zone; `ChainSnapshot` now reads in UTC, and the golden snapshot is recorded
  again from the old chain in UTC. The test passes under UTC, CET, New York and Tokyo, and fails in Tokyo with
  the step-5 fix disabled.
